# Transmogrification: Toggling to Disable or not?

## Post 139101 by Jamey (Turtle WoW Team) [STAFF] — 2025-07-14T16:51:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139101#p139101 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Hi everyone,

Today we'd like to have input of the community regarding a feature that may or may not be implemented, depending on the community's response.

The question is simple:

 **Would you like to see a toggle implemented (Just like "Show Helm" / "Show Cloak") that, once toggled, shows all players around you the way they truly look like. Stripped of any and all Transmogrified gear they're wearing and instead showing their equipped gear?**

Please vote using the attached poll and, if wanted, explain your reasoning by replying to this post.

Please note that a simple majority vote on one of the poll options doesn't immediately mean the outcome will be as such. We will take the poll and replies into account.

## Post 139103 by Runningjohnny — 2025-07-14T17:02:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139103#p139103 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Transmog is player identity. Risk for turning into an Emperor's New Clothes situation if it's a toggle.

<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139104#p139104 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I am not a pvp player but I do find these reasons convincing and believe this solution will be welcome and beneficial.

## Post 139111 by Dannyp92 (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-07-14T17:13:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139111#p139111 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Finally the Turtle WoW devs actually use polling. Kudos! But polls like this should be way more common.

Transmog should be toggleable primarily for two reasons:

 *PvP: Transmog allows players to hide their gear, tricking others into believing they're less geared than they actually are.

 *Immersion: Transmog allows players to have silly, immersion-breaking outfits that don't fit the more gritty, medieval aesthetic of Classic WoW.

## Post 139114 by Manolothegreat (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-07-14T17:17:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139114#p139114 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Some players put a lot of effort into collecting transmogs because they care about how their character looks — and they don’t want to be seen like this:
![Image](https://i.imgflip.com/78v72p.jpg)

<https://imgflip.com/i/78v72p>
I personally wouldn’t want to be seen like this by others. The trasmog is part of the player's identity; it shows your style and personality. Disabling trasmog would lead to every bis player looking the same. It's just bad, a big step backwards for no reason.

While I understand the intention behind this feature, I think the Turtle team has more urgent issues to focus on. There are other things that break immersion even more, such as:
- Odd-looking mounts (like the rainbow cloud)
- Disguises that don’t blend well (like wargens, dragonkins, etc)
- Druids in disguise, making it impossible to tell what form they’re actually in
It might be worth revisiting the priorities here. I vote **NO**

On top of this, this feature would make trasmog useless or pointless. You won't be able to feel elegant because you will know some people see you as a clown. Is just pointless, focus on important things, please

## Post 139123 by Ardaran Feralsoul — 2025-07-14T17:32:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139123#p139123 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Considering that at least in part the most sizable portion of the community is Nordanaar, a *RP*PvE realm, disabling transmogrification is more or less, as others said, not something I would like to see people being opted into by default because its removing no small amount of the fantasy of an MMO*RP*G. I emphasize the roleplaying element here twice because on some level, you are roleplaying, and that's a huge draw of it. Your character is your avatar who you interact in the world with for basically everything. Other people seeing you in your clown set isn't exactly conducive to who you want to present and portray as in the world; even people who do zero roleplaying transmogrify their gear and some go to huge lengths to get it. It is also a milestone in a way, getting to have a fully customized set of armor and weapons you like, not just what the game gave you. If people can just ignore than then why bother it? Just so you feel in your own mind that way? That's an odd choice.

Regardless, I also agree there are "bigger issues" in terms of breaking immersion and game balance (not disabling illusions or disguises, mounts that do not match the art style or aesthetic of the game be them core hounds or happy rainbow clouds and beyond), and that the solutions are simpler. If you are an avid PvPer, while visual information is going to trump numerics at a glance (seeing if someone has 3000 health or 5000 is harder to tell than "big glowy golden paladin armor" versus "I got this from Un'goro Crater 8 levels ago"), you have those tools are more or less need to use them as is or you are just plainly playing at a disadvantage.

TL;DR: Getting to ignore transmog feels pretty against the spirit of anything that's "Classic+" as well as a server that's specifically listed RP and in PvP there's ways around it all while there's a lot more garish examples of things that just feel out of place.

## Post 139134 by -Exaviel- — 2025-07-14T17:49:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139134#p139134 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I vote No for the following reasons:

I totally get the curiosity around seeing someone’s actual gear, but I think adding a toggle like that goes against the whole point of transmog. People put a lot of effort into how their characters look — it’s a big part of how they express themselves in-game. Letting others strip that away with a toggle kind of undermines that.

It also opens the door to more gear-based judgment and elitism. Transmog helps level the playing field in social settings — you don’t instantly know what someone’s ilvl is or what raid they’re in just by looking at them. Bringing back a way to ‘see through’ that feels like a step backward.

And realistically, if someone really wants to see another player’s gear, they can just inspect them. No need for a global toggle that forces appearances to change across the board.

This just feels like a solution looking for a problem, and it risks hurting the personalization and creativity that make the game world feel so alive.

## Post 139150 by Madijun — 2025-07-14T18:20:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139150#p139150 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

For me who loooves PvP it's a definite YES. The ability to see your opponents items in PvP can be vital. Meaning it actually affects combat. And this is my one major concern with playing on a server with transmogs.

I know that many people value the art of transmogging - and that's fair. But I don't believe it should be a factor in actual combat for the people who likes to assess one's opponent partly based on their items.

Therefore, I think it's only fair that the people who value PvP (or just seeing people's actual gear) should have that option, just as the people who value the transmogging (or just seeing people's transmogs) should have that option.

I can't see why this option should be a problem for anyone, since it enables anyone to see the world/characters as they desire.

## Post 139211 by Fundead — 2025-07-14T20:32:00Z

I like the idea that transmog is account based (like addon) and you can choose whatever to look like.  3) As there would be both options for people to play with and without I don't see problem for anybody with that, so everybody should be happy as if you like it - turn it on, if you don't - turn it off... there is no negative effects of it.

I can tell you that really nobody gives a s*it about how you look really in wow and IRL (with age you understand it more and more), you do it for you not others, as they don't care, at that point just use ''transmog on'' option.

## Post 139217 by Kiercy — 2025-07-14T20:38:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139217#p139217 | page 3 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

This seems like a waste of resources to try and implement. This server runs on RP immersion which is why people buy transmog, mounts, etc. from the shop. If this would be implemented what's the point of buying any transmog then if you won't potentially look like that to other players? This seems like a potential decrease to player immersion. If you want to add a feature to the game everyone would want-how about fixing whatever the bugs/errors the hair dresser npc was causing.

Everyone who was there for the 24 hours or however long it was for that NPC was thoroughly excited-so much to the point you couldn't click the npc without clicking another player thats how popular it was. Then we got told it was revmoed due to causing errors and to stop asking for it? We all love the work you guys do and are very grateful for what new ideas you come up with. But can we please backtrack a little bit to something simpler before trying to implement a new system that will come with its own myriad of separate errors/bugs? What's to say this won't just be another hairdresser that turns out to be too much work?

Finally/in addition: This server is posted online as an RPPVE server, this doesn't seem to have those who the server was catered toward at the forefront. I've done the PvP. And as mage I'll say-whether its a naked shaman with a Sulfuras, or a full Earthshatter Shaman with a Shovel-I still get 1 tapped with 4k hp and full frostfire. It's a p-server with custom classes now. If the pvpers are complaning they can't PvP because they can't judge someone's gear....uh have you seen retail....or like 80% of other custom servers? I'm the last person who's going to say it's a skill diff...but like-skill diff'd. Part of the pvp fight in mental as well: If im trying to psyche out my opponent by making myself look weaker then they need to be aware enough to recogonize something is fishy. You can be 1 shot by an arcane mage now too. Fire isn't alone anymore. If anything, if someone believes this change is good for PvP, their not as advanced in it as they think they are. This solely lowers the skill cap ceiling. If you want classic PvP, you cannot find it here as these are not classic classes. A Tmog change that screws the RP community doesn't fix PvP. Bottom line is-Your lack of skill shouldn't ruin my fun. Please don't make it so that PvPers ruin the RP side. I want to be seen as the simplistic elf tmog i have-not a blue glowing popsicle.   scared_turtle_head    -also its not like we have a literal dedicated pvp server you could just remove tmog from or anything >.>

Also-if this is about the Project Epoch release and now the Twow teams feels like they have to push out this feature bc they have it-You guys don't have anything to worry about. People's progress, gear, custom items, friends etc. are all here. Everyone will still stay here or come back after a period of time. The people who you may potentially lose were going to leave regardless of what you guys do, please don't try begging to your ex whos leaving out the door when he was no good for you in the first place hun'.

*Last edited by Kiercy on Mon Jul 14, 2025 10:01 pm, edited 6 times in total.*

## Post 139218 by Valari (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-07-14T20:38:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139218#p139218 | page 3 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

“I really don't think PvP players should be given this much of a platform to have this kind of feature added on a RPPvE server, like... Do the gankers really need to see what the Baby Warmode Turtle they're killing is wearing underneath?”

This is a common consensus on the discord I am on, and honestly if you will only pick fights with people who are weaker than you then I don’t care about your opinion or believe you should have power to change how my OC looks.

While this feature may be being implemented from the devs to curb the naked transmog issues in the community, there are other ways to go about this change such as use of the report tool for immersion breaking. Transmog tokens are a currency, one which many players and especially those in RP guilds have collected and in some cases paid IRL currency for via the donation shop. You do quests for them, do dungeons for them, and they are hard won.

For as many people who do naked mogs, I know five more who chose transmog because they were uncomfortable with how their base gear made their female characters look, exacerbated by the uncomfortable existence of the A Little Extra mod of which they have no opt out feature. They transmog to avoid unwanted sexualization to full armor sets which match their characters, and deserve to be respected with their time in farming honored.

There are better ways to deal with intentionally immersion breaking transmogs, and on a RP based server we should not be stripping roleplayers of their hard won identities or forcing women into uncomfortable gazes they have purposefully transmogged over to avoid.

## Post 139239 by Alcilis — 2025-07-14T21:40:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139239#p139239 | page 3 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

## Post 139240 by Rurin — 2025-07-14T21:43:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139240#p139240 | page 3 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

From the perspective of main, oldest server: NO.

The server is RP at it's core, even if majority of current population probably doesn't care about RP. One of the fundamental elements of RP is how your character presents itself to others. When you want to invest time into looks of your character, but others can just disable it, it partly defeats the whole point.

However, I agree that many players use transmog in the "unintended" way, creating most ridiculous looking characters possible, for reasons completely disconnected from any identity or RP. Those who played Lost Ark or SF6 know to what levels this problem can escalate...

I don't think anyone likes looking at the most ridiculous transmogs around (like that infamous naked gnome). Even players who engage in transmog most intensively.

If any specific items are problematic, deal with that! It often really feels like 80% of the problem are the few very specific items (programmer socks anyone?)

> **Lazare wrote: Mon Jul 14, 2025 9:46 pm**
> > **Nemetth1 wrote: Mon Jul 14, 2025 9:38 pm**
> > Most of you are complaining that this would affect roleplaying. But to me, there's NOTHING less roleplay-appropriate than someone worrying about whether their boots match their helmet in a game about war, death, blood, and honor. You don't want roleplaying—you want to play The Sims: Azeroth Edition. Transmogrification of items should be removed for everyone precisely for roleplaying reasons.
> >
> >  Or not—unless you actually believe that soldiers in war care about whether their belt is as golden as their helmet.
> >
> >  Regards.
>
>  A huge aspect of roleplay is creating a character to look how you want them to -- to yourself and to everyone else around you. It's a large part of the creative process.

Roleplaying entails the assumption of a character's identity and motivations.
Phrases such as "I am a warrior who seeks to save the world," "I am a paladin who fights for the light," or "I am a dark magus intent on destruction" reflect archetypal roles grounded in internal drives, ethical frameworks, or narrative goals.

None of these character constructs inherently suggest a preoccupation with outward appearance or with how others perceive them. On the contrary, concern with external perception undermines the internal coherence of such roles.

While the pursuit of beauty, artistic expression, and aesthetics is valid within creative domains, these elements belong to a different discourse. They do not, by themselves, constitute roleplaying in the traditional or narrative sense.

## Post 139245 by Reddguard — 2025-07-14T21:56:00Z

I see a lot of people voting no because there would be „no point in transmogging anymore“. But that is not true at all. The vote is for a toggle, not for removing transmogs!

There are a lot of really cool transmogs on players I pass, I often think to myself,
wow! That player looks awesome!
A mage in full golden robes or a warrior who looks like a battle-hardened mercenary with his eye-patch.
That increases the likelihood I whisper them and we interact. But sometimes there are players which run around in full bunny costums or are nearly naked, I see no thought put into these outfits. It lowes my immersion for this beautiful fantasy world. In these cases I want to disable transmogs.

Like I said, it‘s a toggle! I will have it turned on most of the time! I vote yes.

Also, the people who will categorically turn off transmogs are not the people you want to impress with your transmog anyway, or?

## Post 139246 by Chion — 2025-07-14T21:57:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139246#p139246 | page 3 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

In response to the poll, I voted "no", as in "no I do not think players should be able to hide my transmog on their client side".

Before I get into my reasons for why, I want to mention that I believe this poll was worded in a way which makes it unclear what it means. I had to think very hard to figure out what "yes" and "no" mean in this case. I also saw confusion that this change would remove transmogs completely, which I understand is not the goal. The poll also has yet not been sufficiently advertised to the active players in-game. (It should be mentioned in the yellow-text announcements which frequently appear in chat.)

Being able to hide other players' transmogs creates a barrier between the roleplay community and PVE community on Nordanaar. Players who might not actively roleplay sometimes find themselves engaging positively with the roleplay community when they stumble into friendly roleplay scenes and feel empowered to have a friendly in-character conversation. Occasions like that create healthy opportunities for non-roleplayers to become casual roleplayers, and these opportunities might be lost if Nordanaar players are disabling transmog for meta reasons. After all, not seeing the outfit a character is wearing creates friction in trying to understand the scene.

As other players have mentioned, if transmogs can be disabled client-side, then illusions must also get the same treatment. However, we know that players can buy fashion coins and illusions with TurtleCoins. Being able to disable a player's transmogs gives less reason to buy fashion coins and illusions, which may have a negative impact on Turtle's ability to stay afloat and to pay staff.

The one place I feel a feature like this could be welcome is on Tel'Abim. While I personally believe that "seeing how good stats someone's armor is at-a-glance" is a strawman argument, if the main argument (in favor of the client-side toggle) is a PVP-focused argument, then this is a change that should really only be made on the PVP server.

In conclusion, a toggle like this would be a bad fit for Nordanaar, and only would harm server and community health.

## Post 139251 by Akos1896 (Grandmaster of Forum PvP) — 2025-07-14T22:03:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139251#p139251 | page 4 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Jamey wrote: Mon Jul 14, 2025 4:51 pm**
> Hi everyone,
>
>  Today we'd like to have input of the community regarding a feature that may or may not be implemented, depending on the community's response.
>
>  The question is simple:
>
>
>  **Would you like to see a toggle implemented (Just like "Show Helm" / "Show Cloak") that, once toggled, shows all players around you the way they truly look like. Stripped of any and all Transmogrified gear they're wearing and instead showing their equipped gear?**
>
>
>  Please vote using the attached poll and, if wanted, explain your reasoning by replying to this post.
>
>  Please note that a simple majority vote on one of the poll options doesn't immediately mean the outcome will be as such. We will take the poll and replies into account.

For me it's a yes.
 1. Seeing what the others wear is a valuable source of information in PVP. Should I approach the opponent? Should I avoid that guy?
 2. TMOG as a concept, in my opinion, should allow us to change the character's outfit to our liking and possibly show our fashion to those who care about that. There is a difference between this and forcing it upon everyone whether they want it or not. F.ex. imagine the guys complaining that they can't take the game seriously when the DPS is hitting a giant dragon with brooms and mugs.

As a concept, TMOG is an option in my eye, not an obligation.
Hence, switch would be cool.

Many roles in RP have a very distinct look. Stormwind Guards, Night Elf Sentinels, etc. This is, per lore, that they are required to look a certain way or maintain a uniform that dictates their role in their chosen society.
If you want your character to look like a hodgepodge of collected gear, that is your choice to make your character match that appearance and their overall outlook on life. That is the great thing about the choice that we already have without this needless "update".

Transmog is already locked behind not only having collected the gear, but farming the tokens to alter their appearance. That is the compromise.

And, again, this server is tagged as an RP server. RP should come first. PVP can come first on the PVP server. Implement it there.

## Post 139258 by Nemetth1 — 2025-07-14T22:11:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139258#p139258 | page 4 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> >  Phrases such as "I am a warrior who seeks to save the world," "I am a paladin who fights for the light," or "I am a dark magus intent on destruction" reflect archetypal roles grounded in internal drives, ethical frameworks, or narrative goals.
> >
> >  None of these character constructs inherently suggest a preoccupation with outward appearance or with how others perceive them. On the contrary, concern with external perception undermines the internal coherence of such roles.
> >
> >  While the pursuit of beauty, artistic expression, and aesthetics is valid within creative domains, these elements belong to a different discourse. They do not, by themselves, constitute roleplaying in the traditional or narrative sense.
>
>   Do you know what character design is?

Yes, I know what character design is and that’s exactly why transmogrification has no place in Vanilla WoW

In a world like Azeroth, gear isn't just decoration, it tells a story. It shows what content you’ve conquered, what role you play, and how far you’ve progressed. When you let players override that with arbitrary fashion choices, you destroy the visual language that gives the world meaning

This isn’t character expression it’s pure ego. A warrior in level 60 raid gear looks like a warrior who’s earned their place. A warrior dressed like a level 20 peasant because they "like the vibe" turns the whole game into a joke

Roleplaying isn’t about looking cool it’s about embodying a role
If you care more about your character’s outfit than the integrity of the world they live in, you’re not roleplaying. You’re playing dress-up

Transmog doesn’t add depth. It subtracts clarity, immersion, and respect for the world’s internal logic. If you want fashion, there are plenty of modern MMOs for that. Vanilla WoW was never meant to be a catwalk

And let’s be honest, this is part of a broader cultural trend. In the 21st century, epic fantasy is increasingly consumed like fast food. stripped of weight, stripped of moral complexity, reduced to aesthetic. We see the armor, the swords, the magic, but not the struggle, the sacrifice, or the mythic structure beneath
Transmog is just one more symptom of this flattening, where what matters is the vibe, not the narrative.
If we care about fantasy, we should defend its meaning not hollow it out in the name of customization.

I don’t want to see that. That’s why I’m voting yes.

I do not understand the idea that someone else being able to turn off your transmog for themselves (having to actually go through the effort to do it) would ruin your own experience. People use clientside mods to change their own character in many MMOs, with nobody else to see their fancy new duds or spell effects. The people who would turn off transmog don't care about roleplaying in the first place.

That being said there isn't really a lot of positives for this change either. People claiming clownsuits are the identity of vanilla/classic isn't worthwhile enough imo, and toggling transmogs but still allowing the ridiculous illusions means PvP isn't in any better a state than it was before. I'm all for more polls concerning changes, but this doesn't seem worth the dev time one way or the other. That's without even considering people spent real money on fashion tokens.

Exactly. The people who are super into their roles will have the appropriate armor. And by that I mean the genuine article, not some fake illusion slapped onto different pieces. Having a guard outfit, having sentinel armor means so much more than just lacklusterly transmogging the hodgepodge of raid gear you're already wearing.

I have civilian outfits on my characters. Like actual robes, and boots, etc. If I'm roleplaying for a non-combat event, I don't want my characters to wear armor that just magically appears more comfortable and elegant. I want them to wear comfortable and elegant clothes!

I still see no reason there shouldn't be a toggle. If you want to seriously roleplay - just wear the armor directly. It's more lore appropriate *and* nobody can turn it off. Win, win.

> **Akos1896 wrote: Mon Jul 14, 2025 10:10 pm**
> I agree. The thread is filled with fake accounts with 1 or 2 responses altogether. Probably 3 guys are pretending to be a crowd.
>  What you wrote is kinda how I feel. TWOW indeed has some RP roots but if we're honest, RP is a niche thing here with a small (but cool) community. And besides, if all RPers toggle this option on, nothing changes for them.
>  Biggest naysayers seem to be those who paid for something cool and now they can't force the others to see that.
>  But I can't really get behind that argument. People buy stuff for themselves, not to get validation for random online strangers (hopefully).

Greetings,

I'm a player who doesn't use the forums because 99% of the time there's no reason for me or a bunch of other people to say anything. We're usually pretty content. We tend not to say a lot because, for most things, there really isn't a problem. This is one of those "this isn't a problem but now it is" scenarios.

This server has a roleplay history and is rooted in roleplay, in fact, it is still marked an RPPvE server (they could just drop the RP from it one day if they wanted to if they didn't clearly think it was that important and or make an actual RP realm and tell everyone else off, neither thing's happened). Roleplaying has been routinely recognized as a legitimate hobby and activity on the server Nordanaar. Part of being a roleplayer is having a character and an appearance to them. Part of the whole point is to dress up the character and do stuff in the open world. If it wasn't, we'd just all be using forums or Discord or something. It is entirely valid that to some extent it should be displayed in the open world.

If people are upset about "immersion breaking transmogs" remove or edit the offending items like the invisible items and I hate to say this but it is true too. There were always highly revealing items that were used by roleplayers back in the day too, as well as silly items, and were your "Ironforge set" or your "Orgrimmar set" basically.

Last bit here, I have paid exactly $0.00 USD for anything here. The most I have ever got was a gift from a charity raffle the guild I am in did where the winner got a donation reward. Even still, anyone who donated for something - even stuff I don't like the immersion breaking illusions and some of the skins or the mounts - are entitled to get to use their flashy rewards. Its theirs and no one should have the power to take that away (barring the obvious where it was wrongly obtained or they broke the rules sort of thing).

In short, PvPers shouldn't be able to invalidate things just for "the sake of PvP balance" because I hate to tell you PvP balance is not great right now and there's a lot of issues bigger out there than if you can correctly identify an 8ft cowman in a dress with two transmogged Thunderfuries from the guy who just fell out of Molten Core and is clinging to a Obsidian Edged Blade like his life depended on it.

Hyperbole aside, no one else should be able to determine how my character appears to anyone else other than me, and a lot of people have that sentiment.

Also, obligatory lol @ "everyone's a bot!" combined with "they're brigading!" type of argument here in 2025. Let's try to be genuine about this.

## Post 139279 by Geeveeohh — 2025-07-14T23:02:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139279#p139279 | page 4 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I will admit I did not read each and every article here but I see a lot of summary about how the look of the gear is integral to being able to PvP. Many complain how it impacts the knowledge of the game and tmog removes that aspect of the game which they claim is integral. I would disagree. One does not know the combat ability of the person they face. Even retail went away from the silhouette differences that was 'important'. The game and server are predicated upon SLOW and STEADY progression as the main draw and that has ZERO to do with gear. A claim of they have fake gear...you can't mog unless you earned the gear anyway so the entire aspect of a geared warrior has to look like a geared warrior is null and void. If I'm a geared up warrior and I want my opponent to 'believe' I'm a peasant...that is my opponent's choice and failure to right click and hit examine. There is ALREADY A MECHANIC TO LOOK AT GEAR INSTEAD OF MOG and it doesn't currently infringe upon the rights of the player to LOOK how THEY want to.

The game already punishes non PvP players by auto flagging us for an accidental click for a friendly buff. There is an entire donation and game mechanic around tmog and a lot of costs involved in making that happen, not to mention above how you still have to find and earn the gear anyway. To allow people to just arbitrarily cancel what your desire for the character is seems like taking away one MAJOR aspect of any game...customized toons.

Add a toggle for PvP servers or such if you need but taking away customization that has cost a lot of people a lot of time, energy, and even money seems very antagonistic towards a major player base on a server dedicated to the very role play aspect.

I voted NO because there is already a means to view it, it would take away the rights of the mogged players, it immediately devalues the entire server, tmog system, role play aspect, and many other items I feel the server itself is predicated upon. This is just another push by the PvP player base to once again give them MORE of an edge on a server not even designed for PvP.

## Post 139280 by Stirlitz — 2025-07-14T23:10:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139280#p139280 | page 4 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I'm very fond of the transmog system, but I do think that players should be able to opt out of seeing other people's transmogs.
For one, there are transmogs that spoil the immersion instead of generating immersion (I'm looking at you, naked ppl with bunny ears), but mostly because tmog is deception and deception is power in pvp. If a guy is running at me wielding a mom like a madman, I'd rather know what to expect. And honestly, I don't even pvp much and don't care, but I have friends who can tell a person's bracers from the other end of WSG, and I know that they do care a lot about it.

So, I'm definitely pro choice.

The argument that people spend time on their tmogs and want the others to see them, is a bit silly to me. Those who care will see them and those who don't care, well, they don't care about your tmog anyway, ppl, get over it.

I believe that the vast majority of people will opt to see tmogs, but there's a lot of people who will feel much better having the option not to. Personally I'll keep it on, so whatever choice doesn't affect me as I love my tmog and most other ppls tmogs, but I think the option should be there for those who don't want to opt in.

Also, talking about this, you should be able to completely disable the feature but also keep it for your character but disable it for others (for pvp) and perhaps even only disable it for enemy players too (although that would probably create issues with loading visuals during mind controls so it's a bad idea). But a show all / show mine / show none choice should be a thing, imo.

All of my old pvp crew with whom I still speak consider tmog the worst thing of twow. They may be retarded purists, but I understand them.

## Post 139294 by Peligroso — 2025-07-14T23:32:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139294#p139294 | page 5 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Gear Instead of Transmog (Especially for PvP)   insidious_turtle

I think it would be a great QoL feature to allow players the option to toggle transmog visibility—particularly for those of us who are deep into PvP.

Let me explain why:

In PvP, information is everything. Being able to quickly assess what kind of gear a player is wearing (e.g., whether it's PvE or PvP focused, what weapon type they’re using, if they’re wearing tier pieces, etc.) can drastically change how you engage with them. Right now, transmogs make that incredibly difficult. You could be facing someone in full conquest gear or someone in random heroic dungeon blues, but if they’re transmogged into clown armor or full T3 nostalgia sets, there’s no easy way to tell.

Toggling off transmog would help in these ways:

Strategic Targeting: You can identify high-value or undergeared targets more accurately in battlegrounds and arenas.

Improved Awareness: You can distinguish specs easier (some weapons and armor types give away a spec at a glance).

Enhanced Learning: For newer players especially, being able to see what gear top players are actually using helps with learning and gearing decisions.

To be clear, I’m not saying remove transmog or force anyone to turn it off. Just give us the option. Just like we can toggle names or health bars, being able to toggle transmog visuals client-side would strike a nice balance between immersion/customization and competitive utility.

This would be a small but impactful feature for PvPers, and wouldn't affect how players look to others unless they choose to view it that way. Win-win.

Thoughts?

## Post 139309 by Loki23 — 2025-07-15T00:34:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139309#p139309 | page 5 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I think this poll should have been split into two parts instead of packaged into one question. The poll asks if transmogs should be toggleable for "yourself" and for "other players". These are 2 different things. YES, transmogs should be able to be toggled for the player themselves, giving them control over how they appear.  NO, they should not be toggleable for other players, as other players should not control how you appear. For the poll as it is packaged, I would have to vote NO then since it is more important to allow players to choose their outward appearance than it is for them to have an easy toggle on/off for an item.

## Post 139310 by Annihilaki (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-07-15T00:34:00Z

## Post 139311 by Horul17 — 2025-07-15T00:43:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139311#p139311 | page 5 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Voted NO. This toggle goes against the spirit of Turtle WoW. Transmog is part of a character’s identity — not just cosmetics, but roleplay and immersion. Letting others bypass that breaks immersion and encourages gear-checking, min-maxing, and PvP avoidance, which doesn’t belong on a server like this. It also risks setting a bad precedent for more “transparency” features that undermine player agency. Keep the focus on creativity and storytelling, not stats.

## Post 139321 by Underdesk — 2025-07-15T01:12:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139321#p139321 | page 5 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

why are we making decisions that center around pvp on a server intended to be for roleplaying?

if i'm playing on a roleplaying server, i want to be roleplaying my character. this involves wearing an outfit that matches what i'm trying to convey. in the case of my main, a night elf priest-- i'm going to be trying to convey that in my outfit. this is a recognizable silhouette that clues other roleplayers in to what i want to be doing, and generally fits the atmosphere of the world through the 'RPG' lens.

people could make the argument that i could just carry my rp gear with me in my bags on itemrack, which is valid, since that's how it was traditionally in the game for roleplayers before transmog (we can all remember our banks being full from this at some point), but the point of transmog in classic is to subvert bag space slots being wasted on cosmetics that could otherwise be used on actual gear and consumes for pve.

if people don't care about seeing my outfit that both fits the world and conveys something to people i'm trying to interact with, why are they on a server that centers around that very thing? if their concern is pvp or authenticity to classic, what are they getting out of being on nordanaar (rp-pve with custom content)?

why are they not on tel'abim, where this toggle would make more sense (even if it is still a crutch, seeing as how pvpers can download nameplates with colored health bars, or look people up on the armory if they think gear is a metric in why they lost or won an encounter)? <https://turtlecraft.gg/armory>

i have not seen a single lick of world pvp on this server outside of max levels whacking warmode levelers. what i do see in every city and out in the open world (without having to seek it out) is roleplayers and folks with turtleRP, most of whom are utilizing the transmog system for its intended purpose to relay information about their characters to others.

i won't care either way if we end up with a toggle (as it filters away people who likely wouldn't care about roleplay anyway), but it is disappointing to those who have worked hard and invested in their appearance.

<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139344#p139344 | page 6 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Knowing how strong someone is in PvP is easily done by seeing how much HP they have with an addon, so I don't think it's that relevant.

If people can simply choose to not see others' transmogs, then transmogging has less of an impact.
I don't think it's just about flexing on people, but it is part of it, and there is nothing wrong with that. Because it is an MMO. The reason why MMO grinding is so addicting in the first place is because other people can see it, why take away the meaning of something that people find fun and fulfilling.

Grinding for a cool 1% drop rate hat or something is cool and it will deter people from caring about tmogs if they know that people are just going to turn them off. "Let people choose what they see" doesn't really make much sense in an MMO, not everything is about you, it's about the shared experience and transmogging adds to that.

At that point, if tmogs aren't an important part of the game anymore, it starts to resemble a singleplayer experience. You might as well just mod your game and character to look however you want with T3 gear or whatever. It undermines the communal identity of the MMO.

Also, it is still an RP server, even if it isn't treated as one anymore. I think WoW has a lot of roleplay elements that people don't think is roleplay. It's fun when people ask you where you got a cool piece of gear or comment on something in-character.
The consistency of people who are going to see your look matters, it's a social game.

Also, I don't think enough people have super crazy looks that it hinders the game experience. The naked gnomes are awful but you almost never see one out in the world, just in Stormwind. I never played Vanilla or anything else so maybe I'm not used to seeing ugly mismatched gear but yeah.

## Post 139345 by Shokisan1 — 2025-07-15T02:14:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139345#p139345 | page 6 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I think you should be able to toggle it if youre inspecting someone to see what theyre really wearing, or maybe in PVP it could be removed for fairness, but otherwise I think gear transmogrification is a deep end game for a lot of players that people put a lot of time into, especially a class like holy paladin that looks absolutely ridiculous in vanilla wow healing gear even all the way through AQ40 (although the new tier armor sets solve this). So, I think being able to permanently remove someone's tmog against their will goes against RP spirit.

It's definitely weird in PVP tho, like, to see a mage with a naxx rogue sword or something, feels off, and a lot of players will do lots of tmog stuff to hide their true power level, bascially everyone does this, so right now a t3 caster could tmog down to nothing and then just surprise 1 shot people (happening all the time rn).

There's also the current criticism that a lot of tmog items are totally immersion breaking, while other people don't mind and they like seeing naked high elves running around in thongs and bunny ears.

I voted No.

Removing one of the deep late game options for players, especially RPers = less reason to keep playing turtle, idk if I'd risk something so game-changing against the RP community when Epoch is around the corner.

## Post 139376 by Afreeta — 2025-07-15T05:30:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139376#p139376 | page 7 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

If you want a normie wow experience then go play on a normie server. This is Twow, a goofy ass custom server. The classes have all been overhauled and the world barely looks the same. Embrace that direction and keep transmogs the way they are. Don't start catering to vanilla purists.

What this really represents is the cultural shift we've been witnessing on this server for the last few years. Turtle wow has a long history with its roots embedded in not just RP, but a "stop and see smell the roses" attitude. Did you know that originally ALL characters had lowered xp rates? That's why it's called Turtle Wow. Transmogs in their current state are a product of that culture. Giving players a chance to more deeply affect the beauty of this world around them. The playerbase has grown a lot since then, especially in the last few years due to a lack of vanilla pserver options. I would hate to see my favorite server damage it's identity to cater to people who want Turtle Wow to be something it was never meant to be.

## Post 139386 by Avendor — 2025-07-15T06:25:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139386#p139386 | page 7 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Let me try to explain why the transmog thingy is kind of a big deal for some of us naysayers from my own perspectives. It's gonna be a little long, I apologize, but bear with me:

First of all, is it vanity wanting to show off? Maybe, I guess? But you're damn right it's the point that I wanna present my character to look the way I like not because an artificial number says the green shirt is better than the white robe, but because I like the white robe and think it works better on a Priest, for instance. Why else would I go through the effort of dressing up? Sure, for myself too. But also for the world around me. Duh.

As for the slutmogs, you realize of course that it's equally as easy to just unequip anything, yes?
Do I cringe when I see bunny ears in WoW? Yes. But they're not on every head, certainly not on mine (it helps that I can ensure that stays that way), and as long as the item exists and is accessible, we're gonna have to deal. And it's not that big a deal either if someone AFKs with a female avatar without any gear on. Whatever. Welcome to the internet. I think I know which crowd actually values the system and uses it actively though.

And then the people who says we are arguing to take away their option. The irony is we're effectively arguing the same point to a different end, because I experience that argument the same way but in reverse. While a toggleable feature sounds good at facevalue as an option you tab in on, and while it's true some use HD patches and such, the difference is that we're nolonger seeing the same world on our screens.
The HD patches may make the world look a little more or a little less Pixar, true, but if I wear a Runecloth Robe, or pick out the style thereof, then anybody who sees me is gonna see a Runecloth Robe and know that is what I'm choosing to display, the HD patch doesn't -change- that.

If the patch is introduced, we can never be quite sure of who is seeing who, and that becomes a nagging voice in the back of the brain of those of us trying to immerse ourselves in the world. It's a disassociation, a dysphoria, a mental block that may be severe or it may be light. It might be easy for the yaysayers to say "get over it", but it's equally as easy for me to go "no u, gtfo to Tel'abim(/some other server that caters to PvP)" and be just as valid in my tastes.

The work I've already put in to pick out the best pieces, to collect gear far and wide across my journies, the tinkering on what might look good on this or that model, would -effectively- be undone, not because the option to see them isn't there, but because there is now a wall of separation between me and anybody who disables it with little to signal who is who. And that not only retroactively cheapens the experience with the transmogs I've picked out and the effort to collect all kinds of gear across multiple characters, it actively disincentivizes me to continue doing so because it feels hollow now. What I see feels like a lie, and I am nolonger quite 100% part of the world anymore.

And RP? Well, good luck getting immersed when you're constantly second-guessing yourself whether or not what you're seeing is actually there. Is it gonna end RP? Probably not, we can work around it. Just like the lack of the function now hasn't ended PvP cuz any current PvPer can and do work around the transmog system (and illusions) as is. But it is a definite bump in the road that leaves me deflated to think about.

And for me, I think about how Nordanaar is categorized (RP-PvE) and how RP was marketed when I first found out about TWoW and I feel like if they're about to rug-pull me now, that aspect of it was basically a lie and a further step removed from the RP roots the server apparently had, right as I'm dipping my toes into the RP community no less. Which is especially insulting when there is a PvP server right there you can go right ahead with. You can argue that view is a little entitled since I put myself and my wants/needs above the PvPers, but you know what? I grant that, and I stand by it.

If this is an RP-PvE server, prove it by actually catering to the RP-PvE crowd, even if that is at the expense of the PvP crowd. Because Nordanaar is not made for the PvP crowd, unlike Tel'abim. Over on Tel'abim, you have every right and my full blessing to turn that mindset right back on me, and I'd argue you're correct to do so, since that server -is- designed with you in mind.

Thanks for attending my TED Talk.

<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139399#p139399 | page 7 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I think there are 3 sides:
 1) those who wants transmog and everyone to see them, those can just keep the option on, as they care about it and maybe others too.
 2) those who just like the option, but would keep them on, as they use transmog but understand that others may not.
 3) those who think it messes up PVP and RP too (bcoz warrior in robes or with some wizard hat is silly and they want to see world for what it is and judge others by their achievements not transmogs), they can turn it off, they don't care about transmog anyway.

I Personally think that transmog in cata messed up wow, bcoz of RP, as RP is about warrior being warrior and mage being mage. I think good middle ground in transmog can only be that you can transmog in same type of gear, so plate for plate and 2h sword for 2h sword, that would keep RP alive. And in same time there is option to turn it off.

Its like forcing me to eat cake, bcoz you like cake, but I want some fruit.. and its so easy for you to eat cake and for me to eat my orange.

And in the end again to all the tranmog and ppl see me with my 1% drop chance hat - nobody really gives a s*it, you think of yourself as too important. (most ppl will prolly not know about that hat), those who cares - leave the option to see transmog on and you can chat with them about the hat.

If you want everyone to see you with those pink robes and hat with 1% drop, just equip it and stand in some public place (thats how its done with every other game without transmog option (in OSRS we call it fashionscape)), ppl with transmog on or off will be forced to see it and thats that.

And about PVP and ppl saying - dont attack undergeared ppl you bastard and watch only stamina, well maybe I myself am undergeared and want to understand what I am dealing with and should I or should I not engage with somebody. And in Bg's if there is a grp of ppl ofc you first want to kill the lower geared players and gear visuals says it all before checking hp, as than there is no need to check hp of 5ppl and decide the target.

## Post 139409 by Spidernelf — 2025-07-15T07:48:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139409#p139409 | page 7 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Nemetth1 wrote: Mon Jul 14, 2025 10:11 pm**
> > **Spidernelf wrote: Mon Jul 14, 2025 10:02 pm**
> > > **Nemetth1 wrote: Mon Jul 14, 2025 9:53 pm**
> > > Roleplaying entails the assumption of a character's identity and motivations.
> > >  Phrases such as "I am a warrior who seeks to save the world," "I am a paladin who fights for the light," or "I am a dark magus intent on destruction" reflect archetypal roles grounded in internal drives, ethical frameworks, or narrative goals.
> > >
> > >  None of these character constructs inherently suggest a preoccupation with outward appearance or with how others perceive them. On the contrary, concern with external perception undermines the internal coherence of such roles.
> > >
> > >  While the pursuit of beauty, artistic expression, and aesthetics is valid within creative domains, these elements belong to a different discourse. They do not, by themselves, constitute roleplaying in the traditional or narrative sense.
> >
> >   Do you know what character design is?
>
>   Yes, I know what character design is and that’s exactly why transmogrification has no place in Vanilla WoW
>
>  In a world like Azeroth, gear isn't just decoration, it tells a story. It shows what content you’ve conquered, what role you play, and how far you’ve progressed. When you let players override that with arbitrary fashion choices, you destroy the visual language that gives the world meaning
>
>  This isn’t character expression it’s pure ego. A warrior in level 60 raid gear looks like a warrior who’s earned their place. A warrior dressed like a level 20 peasant because they "like the vibe" turns the whole game into a joke
>
>  Roleplaying isn’t about looking cool it’s about embodying a role
>  If you care more about your character’s outfit than the integrity of the world they live in, you’re not roleplaying. You’re playing dress-up
>
>  Transmog doesn’t add depth. It subtracts clarity, immersion, and respect for the world’s internal logic. If you want fashion, there are plenty of modern MMOs for that. Vanilla WoW was never meant to be a catwalk
>
>  And let’s be honest, this is part of a broader cultural trend. In the 21st century, epic fantasy is increasingly consumed like fast food. stripped of weight, stripped of moral complexity, reduced to aesthetic. We see the armor, the swords, the magic, but not the struggle, the sacrifice, or the mythic structure beneath
>  Transmog is just one more symptom of this flattening, where what matters is the vibe, not the narrative.
>  If we care about fantasy, we should defend its meaning not hollow it out in the name of customization.
>
>  I don’t want to see that. That’s why I’m voting yes.

People embody their roles LARGELY through character design to be recognizable in an instant. Sure it's easy for a warrior to just be a warrior. But some people don't roleplay overpowered characters that "earned" their gear. They roleplay farmers or simple bandits or even soldiers with strict uniform dress codes. Do you not see how silly it would feel for a farmer to pull up in full raid gear to an rp event?

Turtle WOW is and always has been largely aimed at roleplayers. You do not get to dictate the way other people play or enjoy the game, neither do you get to dictate how they roleplay.

## Post 139445 by Santory — 2025-07-15T09:59:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139445#p139445 | page 8 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I read through a couple arguments, and i feel like maybe i should throw in mine too.

Since the realm is RPPvE, i feel like Transmogs are an essential part of the game. While there might be xmogs one personally does not like or approve, its nothing different than to real life - your personal view should not have an impact on how someone has to dress up. Obviously you can choose to not party up or be in a guild where people dress a certain way, but giving an option to turn it off isnt gonna negatively impact people that choose to use xmogs.

When it comes to the PvP aspect, i was never fond of the fact someone could estimate your "strength" by seeing your gear, therefore having people attack only "safe" targets. If you PvP, you do it no matter how weak or strong your enemy is, and thats the main reason why i voted "No" on this poll.

And what ive read somewhere, with the kinky xmog stuff and similar - what i personally find really disturbing is not so much some weird taste in looks, but all the weird names some people have and havent been flagged for rename. While the average 12y old might not think anything when seeing a half naked bunny (tho questionable as well in a fantasy game), running around people named with anything like "Cock, Dick, Love Feet, Smell Ass or whatever ive came across" is way more disturbing and should have prio over the question wether or not to divert resources into something purely cosmetic.

<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139447#p139447 | page 8 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Absolutely  NO.   They should not be able to toggle this.  It undermines the whole point of having Transmog in the first place.  Why should I bother to work hard collecting the fashion coins in game to customize how I look,  just for someone to ignore my efforts of all the coins I collected and not see my character the way that I chose to present them?  It makes transmog pointless.  why earn those coins or pay money for them in the donation shop?  If you are going to give players customization in the first place,  then I have a right to choose how I look in front of others.  If you don't want me to have customization, and you want to dictate how I look to you, then don't give us custom choices in the first place.  Make everyone's character model look the same and make each class have a specific look that they are stuck wearing.  No choice at all.  Part of the fun of the game is getting to choose these things.  When I create a character on the creation screen,  I choose the hair, the skin tone, the earrings or beard.  Getting to choose a character and their name is part of what makes the game fun.  It makes the experience more personal.  And so on, with the clothes,  it is all part of the experience I'm having as a game player.  Identity is a significant thing in the real world.  So much so, that there are laws in some countries that protect traits related to identity.  Ethnicity, gender identity, etc are often legally protected.  Why??  Because identity is important to individuals.  Now let's talk about clothes.  How a person dresses is a reflection of their own identity and culture they are a part of.  It is an expression of who you are to choose your hair style and outfit based on what you like, and are comfortable wearing.  But we are talking about a video game;  yeah but that game allows us to make a choice of what our character wears,  so you still get to express yourself through the choices of how you are virtually dressing.  If we are going to do customization at all, then you should respect our right to choose here, just as we are allowed to choose how we dress in real life.  to disable transmog, is to ignore the choices that player made in picking their outfit.  Now in real life,  if I dress up in a classy polo shirt and a nice black leather jacket over the top, and that is what I chose to wear today,  you don't get to walk up to me, strip my jacket and shirt off, and put an ugly T-shirt on me with holes in it instead.  You don't have the right to tell me how to dress.  If you do, then its communism.  So why on the game,  should someone be able to disable transmog and see me wearing some ugly gear that doesn't match the rest of my outfit?  If I put the time into making my character look a certain way, then you shouldn't be able to ruin that with the push of a button.  you could say to me "but you are dictating how I have to see you"  and I can say back to you "yes,it's my choice how i appear, and by disabling, you are dictating my choice by making me appear a certain way before you".   Right of choice is in question.  Is appearance really that important on wow?  To some people, no, not at all.  it's all a video game anyways.  As someone in the role play community however, on this role play based server,  I can tell you that, appearance can be far more important to people who are into role playing here.  Some of us are into character design, and every single detail of our character's outfit can feel important to us and maybe even have some meaning in their story.  By turning transmog off, you can cause parts of the character's outfit to no longer match the rest, or appear as somthing that it should not appear as,  which could even change the context of a role play situation if a character is seen wearing the wrong item, thus changing the meaning of what's happening in the story.  If we picked an outfit that that character is supposed to be wearing at the time, we want people to see our character in that outfit, not a mismatch of random gear.  Please remember that this is a role play based server.  the turtle team is usually respectful of role playing on the server,  and the feelings of the role play community could get stepped on or issues could be created if such a toggle were implemented.  Who benefits from this toggle?  pvp'ers?  guess what.  if that geared player over there is better geared than you or has more skill,  you can analyze his gear all day long and know what it is,  but he is still going to totally crush you in pvp in the end,  and you lost to him either way..  and when you lose,  you don't have the excuse of "well he beat me because I underestimated his gear" anymore.  now time to admit your skill issues.  Don't make this toggle change.  it will hurt the rp community more than it will benefit the pvp community.  As for the nearly naked transmogs,  there has to a better work around than that, to prevent it, rather than the toggle.

## Post 139453 by Akos1896 (Grandmaster of Forum PvP) — 2025-07-15T10:19:00Z

> **Foteinos wrote: Tue Jul 15, 2025 10:00 am**
> cant undestand why this option  must be. world pvp? so if you see low geared,you attack if you see high end geared,you just go? or you call your friends? this sound stupid for me.
>  if you pvp with greens and get1-2 shot ,you deserved it.it happened to me many times when i  started  bgs back in the day and i didnt complained.gear up needs time and dedication.skills 2.
>  naked tmog is stupid tho.must be removed.
>  tmog is identity so im against to toggle it off. i support the inspect function for opposite faction if people want to see lvl of armor without be in the same grp

From PVP pov it's more complicated, not just item level.
Many items have special effects which can affect how a fight goes down.
F.ex. you see a shaman or pally with Hand of Edward the Odd and you gotta factor in that the enemy might insta-heal himself every now and then after a proc.
Or you see a goblin rocket helmet in the distance and you know you gotta prepare for that.
Item level is also important but these random effects linked to gear are no joke, too.
Careful reading tells you if you gotta factor in those effects.

Another option. I'm not a fan of it but a possibility:
Creating a potion from really cheap and easy to get ingredients which make you see through tmog for an hour.
Almost the same as toggle but requires a bit of effort.
At PVP you won't go above buff cap anyway, you can just drink an item like this.

I'm still pro-toggle but after Sylveria's entry, I understand the other side slightly better.

## Post 139459 by Allarielle — 2025-07-15T10:37:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139459#p139459 | page 8 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Back in the vanilla WoW days, when I wanted to look a certain way, I had the option of keeping that outfit in my bags and equipping it when I wanted to use it. I used this option frequently and had a lot of fun doing so! In my opinion, this is the best model: it lets RPers RP in their preferred outfit, it doesn't disrupt PvP, and it also builds into the game that sense of progression when you're able to slowly replace your levelling clown outfit with something more cohesive as you reach the peak of your journey. It is my intention to do this on most of my characters, though few of them have enough gold for luxury just yet :P

Personally, I'm of the opinion that transmog should be eliminated entirely, but replaced with an outfitter system that can be set up to hold different predefined outfits and equip them on command - possibly paired with a keyring-like bag to save on bag space. That being said, if transmog is something that people can toggle off, I imagine that setting up equippable outfits will be something that many players do simply for that reason of wanting to be seen a particular way.

Well, but I'm barely a part of this community, so I guess my opinion doesn't count for much :P

## Post 139464 by Battlefervor88 — 2025-07-15T10:54:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139464#p139464 | page 8 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Think of the TurtleRP addon. It allows RPers to write a comprehensive story about their character as well as some other relevant stuff. Only people with the addon can see the RP text. Everyone else is oblivious to it and that's FINE. Not everyone needs to know that you are the cool protagonist in your own head. Tmog should be the same.

Only egotistic people want to force others to view them in a certain way, because they care only about THEMSELVES and THEIR experience of the game. Anyone who has a healthy attitude understands that a checkbox in the game settings cannot hurt anybody, because it's OPTIONAL. If people wanna turn off tmogs on their client, let them do it. Graphic mods are a thing as well and no one complains about those. If I wanna go make a mod that alters character models into clowns, no one can stop me. Freedom of choice should always be prioritized over player ego. Those who like tmogs will keep the setting enabled and be happy. There's nothing wrong with that.

## Post 139478 by Ardaran Feralsoul — 2025-07-15T11:27:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139478#p139478 | page 8 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

So, a few things which probably won't be commented on or answered by anyone.

First, those couple of times a comment about Turtle RP and addons pops up which already alter how your character appears to others, why not just take the logical step and just make it possible for addons to ignore transmog, if you are so committed? Any serious PvPer would do this, or at least enable it in Battlegrounds. Why does the normal populace of a server where transmog is just generally assumed to be fact of life and important identifier of them and their character have to be subject to the whims of other people?

Let's phrase this first one another way. You, yes you, you the one reading this, get to choose what you wear every day. Yes, even if you go to work or school or similar where there's a dress code. You still make choices within that. You, and you're flat out lying if you say you don't, choose to express yourself and present yourself in a certain way. You do it intentionally too. Unless your mother or your wife or significant other or whoever-else-you-have-that-kind-of-relationship-with is dressing you, this a bunk argument. If you're a slob and you're pheromonemaxxing to attract the females, guess what? That's also a choice in appearance. Not a great one, but it exists. Joking aside at some people's expense, this is the same thing here. People, not even just RPers, do this every day - other people don't get to decide what you look like, at most social conventions and norms do (like how everyone here basically universally agrees the naked armor and mature transmogs are pretty distasteful).

Second, if transmog is so immersion breaking to you to see someone as a farmer putting the smackdown on someone who's "defeated Nefarian" in the middle of Arathi Basin and so on, does it not ever strike you as weird that through your whole leveling adventure that a Hillsbrad Farmer can onetap a warrior who stepped out of Northshire Abbey? I mean, that makes no narrative sense does it? A novice, trained fighter versus guy who picks apples and bales hay? How does a giant chicken the size of bear just get to put you in the ground when you're a level 60 druid trying to get Swift Travel Form? He should be part of a family bucket meal, not dropping bodies of people who killed Ragnaros the Firelord. But we all just suspend our disbelief and let that happen.

Third, for gear appearance and NPCs and the idea of "roleplaying your gear you earned" by treating it as "canon", does it never strike you as equally odd that the NPCs wearing what amounts to a bunch of randomly equipped but cosmetically appealing Uncommons and Rares, are they just "built different"? They're just full natty and you're just a juicer who has to be backpacked by your gear that 19 to 39 other people helped you get? I'm going to say they just all have to be that guy and you're not lil bro.

## Post 139481 by Felor — 2025-07-15T11:37:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139481#p139481 | page 8 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

The poll is flawed, this will only get the most radical answers. You should added more options for partial.

Personally I believe in battlegrounds and pvp enabled transmogs should be removed entirely. Understanding gear by visuals is part of the wow pvp experience. Seeing a T3 warrior with Sulfuras in AV was a rallying point. You followed along or ran out if it was an enemy. It's like any pvp game where you see what the enemy is using, and playing the actual game theory that comes with it. Now when all visuals what others are using are hidden, it's just like playing battleship where you have no idea what is going on until someone dies.

For people complaining about seeing people play in transmogs that they dont approve, people are going to do it anyway with or without transmog, it's just going to be a negative of having to carry more gear and being less effective for group content.

>  First, those couple of times a comment about Turtle RP and addons pops up which already alter how your character appears to others, why not just take the logical step and just make it possible for addons to ignore transmog, if you are so committed? Any serious PvPer would do this, or at least enable it in Battlegrounds. Why does the normal populace of a server where transmog is just generally assumed to be fact of life and important identifier of them and their character have to be subject to the whims of other people?
>
>  Let's phrase this first one another way. You, yes you, you the one reading this, get to choose what you wear every day. Yes, even if you go to work or school or similar where there's a dress code. You still make choices within that. You, and you're flat out lying if you say you don't, choose to express yourself and present yourself in a certain way. You do it intentionally too. Unless your mother or your wife or significant other or whoever-else-you-have-that-kind-of-relationship-with is dressing you, this a bunk argument. If you're a slob and you're pheromonemaxxing to attract the females, guess what? That's also a choice in appearance. Not a great one, but it exists. Joking aside at some people's expense, this is the same thing here. People, not even just RPers, do this every day - other people don't get to decide what you look like, at most social conventions and norms do (like how everyone here basically universally agrees the naked armor and mature transmogs are pretty distasteful).
>
>  Second, if transmog is so immersion breaking to you to see someone as a farmer putting the smackdown on someone who's "defeated Nefarian" in the middle of Arathi Basin and so on, does it not ever strike you as weird that through your whole leveling adventure that a Hillsbrad Farmer can onetap a warrior who stepped out of Northshire Abbey? I mean, that makes no narrative sense does it? A novice, trained fighter versus guy who picks apples and bales hay? How does a giant chicken the size of bear just get to put you in the ground when you're a level 60 druid trying to get Swift Travel Form? He should be part of a family bucket meal, not dropping bodies of people who killed Ragnaros the Firelord. But we all just suspend our disbelief and let that happen.
>
>  Third, for gear appearance and NPCs and the idea of "roleplaying your gear you earned" by treating it as "canon", does it never strike you as equally odd that the NPCs wearing what amounts to a bunch of randomly equipped but cosmetically appealing Uncommons and Rares, are they just "built different"?
