## Post 139518 by Reddguard — 2025-07-15T13:31:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139518#p139518 | page 9 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Hmmmmmmmm.

I read all the posts, and there were some arguments for 'NO' that I hadn't considered.

I voted yes because I don't want to have to see silly outfits like the bunny outfits or "slutmogs". These have no place in this fantasy world, they ruin the immersion! (Outside of the occasional event, I guess.)
I imagine those people think it's funny that others have to see their lame outfits.

But if the toggle gets implemented, people who want to do harm this way will probably just equip the real pieces, and I would still have to see it. I have this problem mainly in the cities, where they can wear it safely without worrying about getting attacked.

Another point: I see a lot of veteran roleplayers arguing against the toggle. And I think Turtle WoW should listen to those players first, because this is a RPPvE server (I wish it were RPPvP, but that’s another discussion). A lot of these players have been here for years. It would be bad to aggravate them, because their opinions carry more weight.

But the most compelling argument I read is this:

> **Avendor wrote: Tue Jul 15, 2025 6:25 am**
> While a toggleable feature sounds good at facevalue as an option you tab in on [...], the difference is that we're nolonger seeing the same world on our screens.
>  [...] if I wear a Runecloth Robe, or pick out the style thereof, then anybody who sees me is gonna see a Runecloth Robe and know that is what I'm choosing to display [...].
>
>  If the patch is introduced, we can never be quite sure of who is seeing who, and that becomes a nagging voice in the back of the brain of those of us trying to immerse ourselves in the world. It's a disassociation, a dysphoria, a mental block that may be severe or it may be light.
>  [...]
>  What I see feels like a lie, and I am nolonger quite 100% part of the world anymore.

And I am genuinely afraid of such a tear in the collective experience. It fractures the world, and I would feel less connected with other players.

Thus, I have decided for myself:
 **The right to be seen as I want to be seen is greater than the right to not have to see other players how they want.**

Some said it would be like the TurtleRP addon: people who want it get it, and people who don’t just don’t get it!
But the fundamental difference is that, with the addon, you can see which other players have it as well.
With the transmog toggle, you can’t see who has it on or off.

A possible solution - if the toggle does get implemented - would be to somehow show whether a player has it on or off, e.g., in the tooltip.

That said, I did read compelling arguments in favor of forbidding transmogs in battlegrounds. There, you could cultivate the skill to recognize unique pieces like the Goblin Rocket Helmet or specific weapons with unique proc effects. Then you can anticipate those effects and plan your move around them.

In battlegrounds, the PvP aspect is probably more important than the RP aspect.
Other people say that transmog in BGs is even good for PvP, because it allows for deception. But I think, if you want to deceive other players, you should use consumables like Noggenfogger Elixir, which have a timer and a cost. Then, if you want to pierce that deception, you can try to stall until the consumable runs out or dispel it.

I still have to think about it more,
but I’ll probably land on:
Toggle in BGs — yes.
Outside — no.
(Which, unfortunately, is not an option you can choose.)
Also, it seems I cannot change my vote in the survey.

The resulting challenges (e.g. bunny outfit in a raid) have to be dealt with another way.
Some suggest reporting these players. But where do you draw the line? Then there is one player who looks too slutty for some but others find it immersive. You can't forbid transmogs this way! (only extreme cases).

You could curate every single piece of transmog and flag all the ones who are disturbing (so only these are not shown with the toggle), but wow, who is gonna do that.

Here is an extreme solution: Fully integrate the TurtleRP Addon into the game, make it a glyph like warmode, and everyone can instantly recognise players who have turned it on.
If you have this mode on, you won't have the transmog toggle, you only have it without this mode and in battlegrounds. So you can be sure: If that player has RP mode on, they will see your outfit.
Then I can imagine the report function being useful because it is easier to report someone who is looking silly AND is suppose to RP.

## Post 139519 by Smiteorflight — 2025-07-15T13:39:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139519#p139519 | page 9 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I voted "No" and I tell you why:

On an heavily RP-influenced server like Nordanaar, which thrives on atmosphere, aesthetics, and creative expression, this option could create significant cultural, gameplay, and economic issues.

 1. Roleplay is driven by appearance and expression – not stats
Transmog is far more than just cosmetic fluff. It’s a core aspect of character storytelling. A toggle that strips away someone’s visual style undermines that narrative and reduces RP to a purely mechanical layer.

When I choose how I dress my character, I also choose how I want to be perceived - that’s the essence of roleplay.

 2. Transmog is "digital art"
Just like an artist paints to express themselves - not for Applause - players design their characters with care and creativity.

 - It’s about self-expression, not self-display

 - The visual style builds connection between player and character

 - Showcasing one’s transmog is part of the gameplay loop

A toggle that hides that effort is like locking a painting in a basement: demotivating and disheartening. Guild Wars 2 (for example) calls it “Fashion Wars” for a reason. For many players, visual design is the endgame.

 3. Economic consequences for the server
Transmog items and cosmetic services often serve as economic pillars that support the server. Players invest time, gold, and real money to shape their characters’ looks.

A toggle that nullifies this effort (even knowing the option alone exists) not only devalues creative expression - it undermines the (or atleast one) financial foundation of the server. If a toggle is introduced that hides paid transmog, future transmogrification should be free and players should be compensated accordingly.

In Guild Wars 2, cosmetic purchases make up a significant portion of game Revenue (you can google this fact). This proves that appearance is not a side feature - it’s a main driver of successful MMO models.

 4. Gear transparency is already available
Players can inspect others (often) freely.

 - If you want to know someone’s stats, just check them directly - without breaking immersion

 - PvP happens mainly on a separate server (Tel'Abim) or with warmode on, where gear visibility may matter, but shouldn't for experienced players (and Players opt into warmode entirely by choice on Nordanaar, so any complaints about it seem rather unfounded.)

 - In spontaneous PvE encounters, visual gear has minimal (or no) impact on performance

Addressing Common Counterarguments:
 *“But in PvP, I can't tell how strong someone is by looking at them”*
→ PvP takes mainly a place on Tel'Abim Server (PvP Server). And Players who choose PvP on an RP/PvE server are part of a niche minority, opting into a mode that does not reflect the wider community's focus. That minority should not be allowed to undermine a core element - visual expression - for everyone else.

 *“It breaks immersion when people run around as murlocs or half-naked”*
→ Immersion is subjective. For some, humor is part of the world; for others, it’s nostalgia or narrative contrast. Costumes, illusions, and transmog are intentional features designed to support RP creativity (and surely make money also). A toggle that distorts mutual visual perception does more to break immersion than a silly outfit ever could.

Conclusion:
The “Show True Gear”-Toggle is not appropriate for an RP/PvE server like Nordanaar. It undermines roleplay, diminishes creative freedom, threatens economic stability, and weakens player motivation.

Transmog is not “hiding” gear - it’s expressing character, crafting art, and enriching the world. I'm very sure, that this toggle prioritizes a vocal minority over a broader, quieter community of players who rely on visibility, aesthetics, and style.

Transmog is effort and investment. If you’ve earned or paid for it, your look deserves to be seen how you choose and not forcefully replaced by a switch that the wider playerbase likely never asked for.

## Post 139520 by Elesion (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-07-15T13:39:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139520#p139520 | page 9 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I love this whole debate. There are points being raised that seem utterly insane and alien to me, that would not have crossed my mind in a million years thinking about this issue. So at some point we have to recognize that it boils down to irrational preferences and there's not really a point in having a debate if the starting positions are so wildly different:
 **"I choose how *my* game looks including your character" vs "I choose how *my* character looks including your game"**
Neither of these position is fundamentally superior to the other, it's just a different brand of the same possessive irrationality.

But there is one thing I would like to throw in - the technical aspect. People complain that it would divide the community, that it would create uncertainty how your character looks to others, that it would put people in different worlds.
Brothers and sisters - that ship has long sailed. Every model, every texture, every asset is stored locally in the MPQ files and is subject to modding. People can mod the crap out of their games and it can change the look, the race, the body type of your character, even the feel of the entire world. This is already happening and there is no way of preventing it either. If you curate your character - do it for yourself. Dress up your digital doll anyway you like. But you will never be able to fully control how it looks to others.
Saying a toggle would invalidate transmogging is very silly. I love the transmog system, I have farmed 1% drops just for transmog. And the ultimate goal? It makes me happy looking at my pretty character on screen. I don't care one bit how others see me, because that is beyond my control anyway.

Have High End Gear maybe have some polished/sparkling effect to show things off.
The amount of work this would mean for devs? Probably insane, but we're in the theory stage so I want to share it anyways.
Does every piece of special item need an effect? No. T1 could probably be entirely without effect, while Hand of Edward the Odd or other such things that have a clear obvious sign of a warning signal could have a clear effect, a glowing hand circling the toons hand or similar, it can even be that certain items share the same effect but a clearly differen RGB mixture thrown in to differentiate the T2 shoulderpads to the T4 shoulderpads or whatever.

## Post 139551 by Corpsembo7 — 2025-07-15T14:37:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139551#p139551 | page 9 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Avendor wrote: Tue Jul 15, 2025 6:25 am**
> First of all, is it vanity wanting to show off? Maybe, I guess? But you're damn right it's the point that I wanna present my character to look the way I like not because an artificial number says the green shirt is better than the white robe, but because I like the white robe and think it works better on a Priest, for instance. Why else would I go through the effort of dressing up? Sure, for myself too. But also for the world around me. Duh.

> **Elesion wrote: Tue Jul 15, 2025 1:39 pm**
> Brothers and sisters - that ship has long sailed. Every model, every texture, every asset is stored locally in the MPQ files and is subject to modding. People can mod the crap out of their games and it can change the look, the race, the body type of your character, even the feel of the entire world. This is already happening and there is no way of preventing it either.
>
>  And the ultimate goal? It makes me happy looking at my pretty character on screen. I don't care one bit how others see me, because that is beyond my control anyway.

If one believes it is the sin of vanity and a clearly 100% accurate way to tell someone is egocentric (not saying Avendor believes this, but others do), why exactly do we not just use tmorph or something similar then? It'd be easier, it'd have far more customization, and it would arguably be virtuous as you wouldn't even need to subject other people with your idea of what you think is cool. Sure, it wouldn't be cohesive for groups of people who want to RP, but why would someone who thinks transmog is bad care about those people anyways?

Of course, the fact that most people don't have a version of tmorph means that the existence of tmorph doesn't automatically invalidate everyone who already has tried to transmog. The obscurity and rarity of the existence of someone's hypothetical mod where every armor set is now stormwind guard armor in different colors, even the cloth pieces, doesn't make people give up entirely on transmog. Even mods that are "popular" can count as obscure due to not being included with the base game itself, so it still psychologically feels obscure even if you want to argue that *everyone* totally knows about that-one-mod on this forum. However, if the "stormwind guard armor only" mod was a mere toggle in the options menu instead of being hidden away on one guy's HDD or a forum post? If I can disable everyone's transmog with one simple button click, even outside of a battleground? It's no longer obscure, everyone can be assumed to have these things, and there isn't even the most minuscule "barrier of entry" to prevent it. Why should anyone care about their appearance at that point then?

However, if you only care about how you see your game, I ask again: Why not use tmorph? Why'd anyone even code transmog in the first place if it was just that easy? What was the point of fashion coins besides pissing people off then? The answer would probably be because the idea of transmog is to make players NOT feel like a hallucinating madman who is wearing the most stupid costume ever while insisting that they are totally just wearing knight armor. Developers who care about cheating will say that something like tmorph is bad due to the capability of turning rich thorium veins into huge pillars that can be seen from anywhere, but if random modders can make tmorph, developers could just make a version without the ability to cheat anyways. A player may read this and think that these are all good points, seeing as how players typically have no skin in the game beyond playing it, but legal clientside-only tmorph for all wouldn't benefit roleplayers in the same way that client-AND-server transmog for all does. Furthermore, they'd be more against this if they were developers who have already sold cosmetics and fashion coins. This means one thing: To backtrack on it by just fucking over transmog would be a PR nightmare.

>  I read all the posts, and there were some arguments for 'NO' that I hadn't considered.
>
>  I voted yes because I don't want to have to see silly outfits like the bunny outfits or "slutmogs". These have no place in this fantasy world, they ruin the immersion! (Outside of the occasional event, I guess.)
>  I imagine those people think it's funny that others have to see their lame outfits.
>
>  But if the toggle gets implemented, people who want to do harm this way will probably just equip the real pieces, and I would still have to see it. I have this problem mainly in the cities, where they can wear it safely without worrying about getting attacked.
>
>  Another point: I see a lot of veteran roleplayers arguing against the toggle. And I think Turtle WoW should listen to those players first, because this is a RPPvE server (I wish it were RPPvP, but that’s another discussion). A lot of these players have been here for years. It would be bad to aggravate them, because their opinions carry more weight.
>
>  But the most compelling argument I read is this:
>
> > **Avendor wrote: Tue Jul 15, 2025 6:25 am**
> > While a toggleable feature sounds good at facevalue as an option you tab in on [...], the difference is that we're nolonger seeing the same world on our screens.
> >  [...] if I wear a Runecloth Robe, or pick out the style thereof, then anybody who sees me is gonna see a Runecloth Robe and know that is what I'm choosing to display [...].
> >
> >  If the patch is introduced, we can never be quite sure of who is seeing who, and that becomes a nagging voice in the back of the brain of those of us trying to immerse ourselves in the world. It's a disassociation, a dysphoria, a mental block that may be severe or it may be light.
> >  [...]
> >  What I see feels like a lie, and I am nolonger quite 100% part of the world anymore.
>
>   And I am genuinely afraid of such a tear in the collective experience. It fractures the world, and I would feel less connected with other players.
>
>  Thus, I have decided for myself:
>  **The right to be seen as I want to be seen is greater than the right to not have to see other players how they want.**
>
>  Some said it would be like the TurtleRP addon: people who want it get it, and people who don’t just don’t get it!
>  But the fundamental difference is that, with the addon, you can see which other players have it as well.
>  With the transmog toggle, you can’t see who has it on or off.
>
>  A possible solution - if the toggle does get implemented - would be to somehow show whether a player has it on or off, e.g., in the tooltip.
>
>  That said, I did read compelling arguments in favor of forbidding transmogs in battlegrounds. There, you could cultivate the skill to recognize unique pieces like the Goblin Rocket Helmet or specific weapons with unique proc effects. Then you can anticipate those effects and plan your move around them.
>
>  In battlegrounds, the PvP aspect is probably more important than the RP aspect.
>  Other people say that transmog in BGs is even good for PvP, because it allows for deception. But I think, if you want to deceive other players, you should use consumables like Noggenfogger Elixir, which have a timer and a cost. Then, if you want to pierce that deception, you can try to stall until the consumable runs out or dispel it.
>
>  I still have to think about it more,
>  but I’ll probably land on:
>  Toggle in BGs — yes.
>  Outside — no.
>  (Which, unfortunately, is not an option you can choose.)
>  Also, it seems I cannot change my vote in the survey.
>
>  The resulting challenges (e.g. bunny outfit in a raid) have to be dealt with another way.
>  Some suggest reporting these players. But where do you draw the line? Then there is one player who looks too slutty for some but others find it immersive. You can't forbid transmogs this way! (only extreme cases).
>
>  You could curate every single piece of transmog and flag all the ones who are disturbing (so only these are not shown with the toggle), but wow, who is gonna do that.
>
>  Here is an extreme solution: Fully integrate the TurtleRP Addon into the game, make it a glyph like warmode, and everyone can instantly recognise players who have turned it on.
>  If you have this mode on, you won't have the transmog toggle, you only have it without this mode and in battlegrounds. So you can be sure: If that player has RP mode on, they will see your outfit.
>  Then I can imagine the report function being useful because it is easier to report someone who is looking silly AND is suppose to RP.

Mods exist, and you have no way of opting out of it.
If someone uses the high quality models, the world looks completely different than what you are seeing and it doesn't change a thing for you. Toggling off transmog is the same.

There is no right to be seen the way you want to be seen. If you run around like a bunny whore, you will look to me like a sick weirdo, and there is absolutely nothing you can do about that.

## Post 139611 by Geeveeohh — 2025-07-15T16:36:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139611#p139611 | page 11 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Turtle wow is a role play server, based on the premise of expanding LORE and the STORY of the game. So many people saying how they should be able to change how -I- look. Go into Walmart and ask everyone to dress different because you don't wanna see bunny ears or neon green hair in walmart. Good luck. The fact that a large piece of the player base is coming in and voting for other people's expressions to be suppressed isn't a big surprise in this day and age. The fact they claim it ruins 'immersion' to the game...uh, holiday outfits and such have ALWAYS been part of the game. So spare us all your 'immersion' theory. Every t3 player should NOT be bound to only looking like t3 when questing, dungeon delving, or otherwise.

PvP players already have WAR MODE, Level 1 LUNATIC, PvP Zones, BGs, Arenas, an automatic PvP flagging system that, from what I can see, causes 90% of the fights and frustrations of the game when PvPers come to 'troll' (Sorry troll players) people that do RP. Now you come asking to give them the ability to simply 'turn off' our money, time, effort, and immersion because they don't wanna look at gear other than the raid or PvP gear they earn? Adding this toggle will ultimately remove the freedom of expression from your game. It will remove personalization of people's experience and destroy the server economy and general feel in a dramatic way.

And this isn't only about Role Players. There are a lot of players PvE AND PvP that do enjoy a transmog and want to look and feel a certain way and you remove that? As mentioned, the time, energy, and even money that has gone into a character (Most cases multiple characters) would quite simply be theft of their efforts. I understand the desire to not look at crazy outfits...but if there are outfits that present adult materials...well they wouldn't be in the game as such isn't allowed by ToS. If the devs build and release an outfit THEYT HAVE ALREADY APPROVED IT FOR GENERAL AUDIENCE so if it offends, it is a personal offense not one in violation of game rules.

The vote on this poll should be a hard NO. A vote to allow this toggle is a vote to shift the server away from Role Play and from the donations predicated upon this system. It would harm the server, the community, the game and your primary player base. STOP giving the PvP players everything! We came for a ROLE PLAY environment not to constantly be forced into PvP. That is what PvP servers are for. I would vote that instead of this you add a toggle for PvP so that no matter what you can't be forced into PvP. It would reduce trolling, it would reduce fights and it would give your PvP player MORE of a reason to go to a PvP server where this isn't an issue. Stop taking our role play communities and turning them into PvP centric areas!

> Mods exist, and you have no way of opting out of it.
>  If someone uses the high quality models, the world looks completely different than what you are seeing and it doesn't change a thing for you. Toggling off transmog is the same.

Sure, mods exist. It might even look completely differently than on my screen.
But at that point, it's they who have gone out of their way to have that be the case and I can reasonably expect that to be an exception, not the rule. If you make "disable transmog" a standard feature, I nolonger have that barrier between me and the people I see running around, even if one of them are revealed to mod their client so I look like a naked Draenei. This is why adding it as a feature is not a victimless change, which yaysayers have to acknowledge to even if they think it's worth the cost if they want to argue the case honestly.

> There is no right to be seen the way you want to be seen. If you run around like a bunny whore, you will look to me like a sick weirdo, and there is absolutely nothing you can do about that.

Between the two of us, I think I've sacrificed a bit more for my privilege on this here RP-PvE server.

And for the record, I've yet to actually meet or interact with anyone who uses slutmogs. I see them around now and then in Stormwind and Ironforge of course, but rarely ever out in the actual world. It's not a majority of transmoggers by any means, let alone the most dedicated users of the feature.

> >  If someone uses the high quality models, the world looks completely different than what you are seeing and it doesn't change a thing for you. Toggling off transmog is the same.
>
>   Sure, mods exist. It might even look completely differently than on my screen.
>  But at that point, it's they who have gone out of their way to have that be the case and I can reasonably expect that to be an exception, not the rule. If you make "disable transmog" a standard feature, I nolonger have that barrier between me and the people I see running around, even if one of them are revealed to mod their client so I look like a naked Draenei. This is why adding it as a feature is not a victimless change, which yaysayers have to acknowledge to even if they think it's worth the cost if they want to argue the case honestly.
>
> > **Soraownz wrote: Tue Jul 15, 2025 4:21 pm**
> > There is no right to be seen the way you want to be seen. If you run around like a bunny whore, you will look to me like a sick weirdo, and there is absolutely nothing you can do about that.
>
>   You can argue I have no right to have my character look the way I want, I can argue you have no right to undo the aesthetics I've worked up and paid with effort and in-game currency over the course of.. two or so years thereabouts to have. Between the two of us, I think I've sacrificed a bit more for my privilege on this here RP-PvE server.
>
>  And for the record, I've yet to actually meet or interact with anyone who uses slutmogs. I see them around now and then in Stormwind and Ironforge of course, but rarely ever out in the actual world. It's not a majority of transmoggers by any means, let alone the most dedicated users of the feature.

Yeah and just like how someone needs to get out of their way to use their mod, so does someone need to go out of their way to toggle transmog off. I think it should be on by default, but people that want to opt out should be able to.

Having that toggle absolutely doesn't affect the people that have it turned on in any way. It doesn't change how you look, it doesn't change how everyone else looks to you. And the way you look to others doesn't matter because you don't have agency over another persons game.

You have a right to decide how your character looks. But your agency ends where it infringes on the options of other people.

## Post 139667 by Avendor — 2025-07-15T18:32:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139667#p139667 | page 12 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Soraownz wrote: Tue Jul 15, 2025 5:18 pm**
> Yeah and just like how someone needs to get out of their way to use their mod, so does someone need to go out of their way to toggle transmog off. I think it should be on by default, but people that want to opt out should be able to.
>
>  Having that toggle absolutely doesn't affect the people that have it turned on in any way. It doesn't change how you look, it doesn't change how everyone else looks to you. And the way you look to others doesn't matter because you don't have agency over another persons game.
>
>  You have a right to decide how your character looks. But your agency ends where it infringes on the options of other people.

I've explained elsewhere in the topic how it does, in fact, affect me on a mental and motivational level; and yes, it *does* change how I look, that's the point. Maybe not on my screen, but it does on other people's screens, so I can't trust what I'm seeing anymore. I can't pick a look and feel like "this is me" anymore if there is a 50/50 chance that said look doesn't exist to anybody else.

As for having a right to decide how my character looks, well. Apparently not. That's the crux of the argument, and this is why I say we argue the same thing from opposite directions. For me, the "agency" to toggle transmogs off infringes upon my ability to choose my own aesthetic, and whether or not I know if X person in front of me has it toggled on or off, it affects me because now I'm constantly aware that I might look completely different on someone else's screen, and that is a layer of separation between me and person X, of me and the world around me that I want to be part of.

Suddenly the choice is not mine how *I* look out there in the world, but yours times insert playerbase numbers minus one. You don't wanna see what I choose to look like? Well I don't want you to see what I don't choose to look like. wawa.

We can agree to disagree here, but do acknowledge that the change, if it happened, does have an effect upon tmoggers like myself, and that's why there is a fervent counter-argument against the idea of a toggle that isn't just pure vanity or some conspiracy to take control over you, just like I acknowledge you're arguing your case because you think it's the better outcome and not because you want to control my character's ability to wear whatever. Not doing so is dishonest at worst.

## Post 139680 by Manolothegreat (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-07-15T19:24:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139680#p139680 | page 12 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

This is my second post in this thread, but I thought of a real-world analogy that might help explain my perspective:

Imagine someone invents a pair of glasses that can see people naked through their clothes. Most of us would agree that's a huge violation of privacy, right? Because it shows you in a way you didn’t choose to be seen.

Now imagine the opposite, someone invents glasses that automatically "cover up" people wearing short skirts or crop tops, because some folks feel uncomfortable. Again, you'd be changing how someone chooses to present themselves. You’re not just affecting your view, you’re rewriting someone else’s identity because it makes you uncomfortable.

Now, the toggle function would work exactly the same as either of these glasses. You're not just adjusting your experience; you're choosing to rewrite how someone else appears, regardless of how they want to be seen.

In-game transmogs are more than fashion; they're how we distinguish ourselves. Without them, most classes end up looking exactly the same in tier sets or meta gear. Removing someone else's transmog is like saying, "I don’t care how you want to be seen, I want to see you the way I prefer."

I respect that not everyone likes all transmogs. Some may find certain styles too much. But personal discomfort shouldn't outweigh another player’s right to self-expression in a fantasy game built on customization and creativity.

Let people be seen the way they want to be seen. That’s what makes this world immersive.

Thanks for reading.

## Post 139720 by Manolothegreat (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-07-15T22:17:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139720#p139720 | page 13 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Jtn wrote: Tue Jul 15, 2025 7:31 pm**
> If you want to apply real-world logic to this, then let's talk about how transmog is like applying photoshop and a lot of filters to photos to deceive/catfish other people.

Transmog is NOT like Photoshop or filters—it's just clothing. And in this game, people choose their gear based on stats, not looks, so transmog becomes a core part of a player's identity. Without it, we’d all end up looking exactly the same, because BiS gear is usually identical across players of the same class/spec.

To summarize, the main arguments from the “yes” voters seem to be:

- PvP – The brave PvPers claim they need this to identify paladins with MoM so they can avoid them, while focusing on undergeared warriors in UBRS gear. If that’s truly the concern, then make the toggle work only in battlegrounds and arenas.
Also, keep in mind: this toggle will NOT remove illusions—so you still won’t know if that green dragonkin is going to smack you with MoM or a rolled-up newspaper.
And let’s be real: illusions mostly come from the cash shop. If they want to make them toggleable for competitive clarity, they’d need to offer a lot of refunds.
- Slutmogs – I play Horde, and the only time I even see these is when I log into Stormwind on an Alliance alt. It’s hardly a widespread issue. If this really bothers some players, the devs could put minimal effort into fixing “naked” transmogs specifically, instead of implementing a full toggle system.
- Immersion breaking – I won’t even go into this one, considering the number of weird mounts, toys, illusions, and effects already in the game.
As for me:
I play a male orc clothie, and I don’t want to be seen as just “an orc in a dress”
But guess what? My BiS chest is... a long dress.
What would be next? Toggle off names?

Anyway, I was just expressing my thoughts. I don’t really care if this gets implemented or not, so save the quotes. But I honestly think it’s a waste of time and effort from the devs. There are way more important things they could be working on instead of this nonsense.

*Last edited by Manolothegreat on Tue Jul 15, 2025 10:46 pm, edited 1 time in total.*

## Post 139723 by Zeedeo — 2025-07-15T22:27:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139723#p139723 | page 13 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Confusing for there to be such an uproar from the RP community, as this would not impact their role playing in any way. Also confusing to see the popularity of the "something being forced on us" argument from that side when that's exactly what "Yes" voters currently feel towards the skimpy transmogs that are so popular (are you sure this drama is actually about protecting RP?).

Also, while Nordanaar is a RP Realm, it feels disingenuous to paint the playerbase as so RP oriented. The realm regularly spikes to over 9,000 players online, and to act as though a majority of those aren't simply treating this as a PvE realm ignores the historical reality of RP being a tiny sub-section of the WoW community.

## Post 139724 by Manolothegreat (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-07-15T22:28:00Z

You say you don't want any of the glasses and don't care how others look, but then you say you'd like to see their real gear in case you fight them. That’s basically you choosing the first pair of glasses in my analogy, the ones that strip away the layer someone chose to show, just so you can see what's "underneath" for your own benefit.

Let’s be real: it sounds like you just want to know if someone’s worth ganking or if you should run. And hey, that’s fine, just call it what it is. Also, in most PvP situations, the only time you can really inspect someone’s gear is before the fight. Sure, you might spot a MoM or T3 shoulders at a glance, but that’s about it.

Aaaaanyway, like I said earlier, I don’t really care if this gets implemented or not. I just shared a random thought in a random thread, and I still think this whole thing is kind of a waste of dev time.

I'm very torn by this to be honest. I love the self expression, of course, and as a big RPer - it's sort of a must for quality RPing.

That being said. . . Vanilla is wonderful in seeing what people have earned, and gauging someone's power or achievements. Inspecting people to see their true gear is a work around that is live now, but it is not always practical like in PvP.

I think having the option to toggle it almost needs to be defaulted to Transmogs ON everytime you long in since that is the current default (and easier to go back if community generally agrees that this was a mistake).

As of this post, 395 YES, 475 NO, pretty close. but 446 replies! this is clearly contentious. I may vote no, but if devs want to tread lightly, default to no (no need to reverse a change you don't make!)

## Post 139778 by Voodoochile (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-07-16T02:52:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139778#p139778 | page 13 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Daoc2001 wrote: Wed Jul 16, 2025 12:58 am**
> As far as PvP goes, knowing the relative "power level" of another player on initial contact by simply looking at them is pretty contrived & basically hand-holding stupid-mode PvP.  But, that's MMORPG PvP for you in a nutshell anyway IMO.  Might as well have everyone flagged PvP with their gearscore rating hovering over their head if you want that sort of simple-minded lameness in PvP.

I... I spent a long time rereading this to make sure I understood correctly what you were saying, because it is just so bafflingly dumb. Most pvp games have ways to visually gauge how strong your opponent is. In League of Legends your target is selected and their player portrait appears on the screen with all of their current inventory items. Quake Champion you can see what gun they have in their hands and know if they are going to fk you with a railgun or get bent over with the normal shotgun. PUBG and most battle royales show you the tier of armor the person is wearing AND the weapon in their hand just by looking at them. Team Fortress 2 shows you even the weapons that passively give bonuses on your character so you know how strong your enemy is at a glance.

With transmogs right now you can't know that your enemy has a Might of Menethil when the game only shows you his tmog of Twig of the World Tree

Having the info to know whether to commit to a fight or not to is so essential in pvp games that it is genuinely unbelievable that such a dogshit take like this can even exist.

*Last edited by Voodoochile on Wed Jul 16, 2025 3:23 am, edited 2 times in total.*

## Post 139780 by Dramhippie — 2025-07-16T03:12:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139780#p139780 | page 13 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Players using transmog to manipulate the look of character models and display inappropriate and lewd looks on their toons glitching the model to do things like show their model with no underwear should just be punished on a case-by-case basis. RP should be fine but Classic WoW was rated T for Teen in the states. Not sure what that implies in the EU but I have a feeling that doesn't mean forcing others to look at your lewd glitched unintended character model.

With PvP, it feels like the player should know what they're getting into when enabling or engaging with PvP and it seems a simple fix might be making it so you can see what a piece of gear transmogged actually is upon inspecting the toon perhaps without forcing the toon to look unintendedly against how the player intends them to look at all times as it seems this toggle feature would? I might be trivializing the process of brainstorming and adding a game feature like this and how difficult that might be for the TWoW team but it does make me think that this whole toggle to disable or not discussion just opens up doors for new discussions for ideas on changes to certain game system overall as far as the PvP aspect is concerned.

Either way, I'm not an RPer and I care how my character is visually presented to other players. Not to roleplay, but for my own enjoyment and immersion since the best gear doesn't always look the best or just so happens to be BiS and/or what 99% of the players in my spec are wearing. Plus it lets you highlight the new content of a Vanilla+ server like this when the new loot from that new content doesn't happen to be best in slot.

Here is a fact: No one cares about your character except you. If you really think people will be toggling tmogs off to "tear" off your clothes and "changing your character's body for perverse reasons", then you have serious issues.

Also, RPers are a minority on Nordanaar. For a huge majority, it is the most popular PvE realm. If TurtleWoW split Nordanaar in a Normal and RP realm, you can best believe the RP realm would be almost empty. It is true that not all "no" voters are RPers, but a lot of them are just people thinking this is a vote to remove tmogs entirely, or they think tmogs will be meaningless. Most of the server do not care to vote and the people afraid of not being able to force their views on others (the "no" voters) are the most vocal, hence why they got more votes on the poll. This poll is mostly pointless anyway.

Lastly, there are old players that have played for years that are also voting "yes" for this change, so saying that the "no" voters are the "real" players, that only they know the "consequences" and how things will turn out is just completely false and arrogant.

Selfishness, arrogance and egotism are very prevalent in many of the "no" voters in this poll. Many people (not all), are thinking only about themselves, which is a behavior that shouldn't be encouraged or rewarded.

I personally like transmogs, and use them, but I am not going to force others in playing or seeing the game the way I want. It is not that serious.

In League of Legends your target is selected and their player portrait appears on the screen with all of their current inventory items. Quake Champion you can see what gun they have in their hands and know if they are going to fk you with a railgun or get bent over with the normal shotgun. PUBG and most battle royales show you the tier of armor the person is wearing AND the weapon in their hand just by looking at them. Team Fortress 2 shows you even the weapons that passively give bonuses on your character so you know how strong your enemy is at a glance.
> >
> >  With transmogs right now you can't know that your enemy has a Might of Menethil when the game only shows you his tmog of Twig of the World Tree
> >
> >  Having the info to know whether to commit to a fight or not to is so essential in pvp games that it is genuinely unbelievable that such a dogshit take like this can even exist.
>
>   Vanilla wow is completely unlike those games, and players have many different builds to choose from in the talent section when paired with gear and buffs. Check HP as a starter, and don’t only attack people weaker than you. That’s not proper PvP.

No, Vanilla is NOT unlike those games. In vanilla you can see if a Warrior is using Hakkari Warblades or R14 swords at a glance and know it is true. You can see if a Shaman is wielding the axe off Emperor Thaurissan or Dark Edge of Insanity and know that what you see is true.

Your opinion on what "proper" pvp is is inconsiquential when a fresh 60 doing AVs picks what he thinks is a decent chance at an even 1v1 and he gets literally 1 shot by a human paladin wearing the Robes of the Exalted and Demonshear when in reality he is wearing Plated Abomination Ribcage and the Corrupted Ashbringer

## Post 139799 by Corpsembo7 — 2025-07-16T06:23:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139799#p139799 | page 14 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Avereth wrote: Wed Jul 16, 2025 6:01 am**
> The more I see people screeching they need to control how you see them, the more I know that a yes vote is the only answer, if only to deny them that power they so badly covet.  Let the people have a silly toggle to be immersed in a more vanilla appearing game if they want to.  I'm an avid tmogger myself but outrage at such an option seems more revealing of people with control issues and personality problems than anything substantive.
>
>  It would actually be really funny if someone released an addon that let you hide tmogs and took its fate out of such a silly discussion.

While no one is perfect from not doing or desiring something out of spite, eagerly expressing in public how you want things to be done just to spite anyone is a poor display. You sound like a contrarian who would be against Stop Killing Games because of how so many people care, no other reason. You may argue that games being killed is different from a transmog toggle, but the logic is the same in any situation like this. To someone who cares about the toggle, it would bother them if the toggle was added, but it wouldn't bother someone who didn't care about the toggle. To someone who cares about games, it would bother them if games kept getting made unplayable, but it wouldn't bother someone who doesn't care about games. These are both different situations, but with the logic you have for wanting a transmog toggle, the difference probably doesn't matter for you.

A mod's existence does not psychologically feel the same as a toggle. If it was, I think WoW modding's history would be a lot more controversial and even more punished than it already is on retail. It's not really a gotcha to wish someone made a mod to totally own those stupid people who care so much unlike you, the enlightened centrist, who does not care however would find it really funny if the people who did care were taken down a peg or two.

## Post 139816 by Annihilaki (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-07-16T09:32:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139816#p139816 | page 14 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Avereth wrote: Wed Jul 16, 2025 6:01 am**
> The more I see people screeching they need to control how you see them, the more I know that a yes vote is the only answer, if only to deny them that power they so badly covet.  Let the people have a silly toggle to be immersed in a more vanilla appearing game if they want to.  I'm an avid tmogger myself but outrage at such an option seems more revealing of people with control issues and personality problems than anything substantive.
>
>  It would actually be really funny if someone released an addon that let you hide tmogs and took its fate out of such a silly discussion.

If this actually happened, not even the "Yes" people will get the addon. From the beginning, this debate wasn't really about just not liking to see the transmogs - it's about you not liking transmog in vanilla. But transmog has been a part of Turtle for years, and the game designs resolve around it, with all its **goods** (having to farm and collect mogs, creativity, etc) and its **bads** (random ridiculous mogs, too many slutmogs, etc).

The yes-people (most of them, not all) want the transmog to be fully gone, and they KNOW that by adding the toggle, transmog will completely lose its value. Don't joke yourselves, that's what you really want, and you would vote for this exact thing if the vote was just for that (which in a way, it is).

But while we are at it, why isn't someone of the so "annoyed" *yes*-people making a mod to replace the non-Catholic Church approved skins into vanilla models? I know the *no*-people did just that for themselves- with the bikini mod and the "extra" bodies mod. Those two are actually some of the most popular mods in the mod section, to the point that a big portion of the playerbase uses them. It is a client-side modification, and it doesn't force others to see what they want to see.

We are not arguing about adding a new feature here, we are arguing for essentially completely removing an already well established one. And for the last time, yes, ***removing***. If the option is in the game, and not via a mod, it will make transmog feel utterly useless, to the point it is ditched by players, making them going back to using alt sets from inventory with (armor rack anyone?), as we used to do back in vanilla.

## Post 139833 by Xudo (Grandmaster of Forum PvP) — 2025-07-16T11:18:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139833#p139833 | page 14 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I havent read all 14 pages of this thread, but from replies of smart people I come to conclusion that illusion and tmogs are bad for pvp because they conceal information about your enemies.
As any imbalanced pvp feature, it is bad mostly because no tools to counter it.

The idea of concealing some information is not that bad. Sun Tzu in his book Art of War stated "All warfare is based on deception". So why not?

Just add tools to reveal true power of your enemies. It might be some elixir of "true vision", additional feature of scope.
Some minor limitations for illusions might work too. For example cooldown on usage or reagent consumption.

One more thing to consider. Archeage had option to force everyone to use same character model. Difference was only in color: friends are blue, enemies are red (or something like that).

> > > I... I spent a long time rereading this to make sure I understood correctly what you were saying, because it is just so bafflingly dumb. Most pvp games have ways to visually gauge how strong your opponent is. In League of Legends your target is selected and their player portrait appears on the screen with all of their current inventory items. Quake Champion you can see what gun they have in their hands and know if they are going to fk you with a railgun or get bent over with the normal shotgun. PUBG and most battle royales show you the tier of armor the person is wearing AND the weapon in their hand just by looking at them. Team Fortress 2 shows you even the weapons that passively give bonuses on your character so you know how strong your enemy is at a glance.
> > >
> > >  With transmogs right now you can't know that your enemy has a Might of Menethil when the game only shows you his tmog of Twig of the World Tree
> > >
> > >  Having the info to know whether to commit to a fight or not to is so essential in pvp games that it is genuinely unbelievable that such a dogshit take like this can even exist.

>
>  Your opinion on what "proper" pvp is is inconsiquential when a fresh 60 doing AVs picks what he thinks is a decent chance at an even 1v1 and he gets literally 1 shot by a human paladin wearing the Robes of the Exalted and Demonshear when in reality he is wearing Plated Abomination Ribcage and the Corrupted Ashbringer

I personally would have prefered blizzard makes good looking gear instead of allowing us to tmog old stuff back when it was implemented, but players can now look how they like. Which is a big plus.

Hi, thanks, great responses, question about all that.

My character's a druid, my character dresses like a druid. In fact, I transmog him to look more like a druid than the regular gear you get, especially for us druids who are not always in caster form and choose not to be Balance or Restoration druids because the original artists of the game only really considered them when they designed the sets. In fact, I purposefully transmog his gear to look like the regular nobody NPCs in the game, many of which share the same set of items I choose from. I don't like how much tier sets break immersion or just the clown suit in general.

Where is the "entitled" bit?

Sure, not everyone does that, but let's loop back around again.

People get to choose what they look like, to an extent, in real life and you just have to deal with it. And, for basically everyone functional who's touched grass or seen sun in the past 24 hours, do deal with it. Again, I get this is a game but where is the "entitlement" angle coming from? What's *entitled* is telling someone else how to dress, how to appear, and how to present. Or, relevant here, proposing an option to just negate it. That's pretty entitled to think that's a reasonable power for someone to have. Also, blah blah blah, obligatory "but addons can change it tho." Yeah, I'm not happy with those either, but those aren't from Turtle and the community can't (not really) stop them.

Someone said something like this earlier and I really liked that take.
We have two sides.
One says: My character is integral and I have the right of enforce how people perceive it.
The other says: My game experience is integral and I have the right to enforce how I perceive it.

It boils down to the individual right of self-representation (choosing no) vs. the individual right of free personal experience (yes).

I'm definitely in the yes camp.
Both sides have merits but I think meddling with others' game experience and character perception is more intrusive then letting the individual freely choose the optics.

+ We're pretending that half of the playerbase would toggle it off. Maybe 5% or even less. Vast majority would share the same perception.

*Last edited by Akos1896 on Wed Jul 16, 2025 2:04 pm, edited 1 time in total.*

Transmog literally isn't special privilege or special treatment, neither is the expectations other peoples see it. If you wanted to make that example, it'd be valid if you could only access transmog by donations. Because then *everyone has to* see it and because now you're getting special treatment at the very least, because it isn't something you can earn in game. Do you "inherently deserve" to be seen? Absolutely. Believing you *inherently deserve* at the click of a button to get to ignore and negate other people's appearance is well, pretty entitled.

## Post 139915 by Ardaran Feralsoul — 2025-07-16T15:15:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139915#p139915 | page 15 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Elesion wrote: Wed Jul 16, 2025 2:41 pm**
> You know what? I kinda like the one on the left. It's soulful, authentic, individual, nostalgic of 2005. It looks like an adventurer.
>  Right one is a perfectly acceptable look, but for a feral druid fighting the minions of the firelord the vibe doesn't really fit. It's a great event or city/social transmog, but it's hardly adventuring gear.
>
>  Anyway, your personal preference is completely valid. But it's exactly that - a *personal* preference. Someone else might have a different one :)

It might be authentic to when people were rotating healers off to regenerate via Mp5 but I'm going to have to disagree the character looks like an adventurer. Let's pretend I do turn off the silly cowl and helm, which while we're at it, hey why are other people not allowed to see *those* if I turn them off? Don't they need that too for *their* immersion?

A silly jab aside, this doesn't look like any adventurer or mercenary I can think of in the game. Certainly doesn't look like any night elf druid either, definitely not part of the Cenarion Circle at that. If this is what the rest of the World of Warcraft sees from the eyes of NPCs, I can get why Fandral Staghelm hates all of us whenever we do a quest for him and tells us to, mostly politely, drop dead at the end of each of them. And also pretty strongly passes the check of "adventuring gear" considering no small body golden dragon nameplate boys are basically wearing Poor quality items and they seem to beat down pretty well (throw back to my post about this a couple pages ago).

Anyway, might match the *nostalgia* vibe check but it sure doesn't match the immersion one. Nostalgia's fine, why servers like this even exist, let's be honest. But its not immersive at all unless by immersive you mean being immersed in the imagination of the game itself being meta and the immersion is the MMORPG, not the world of it.

But I can't and won't agree that its somehow an improvement. Stormwind is already a nightmare of riotous colors, gambling gnomes, /yell spam, and quasi nude characters with bunny ears. I don't think it needs to somehow degrade further. I say that cheekily since my entire issue is people shouldn't have a choice, let alone *a vote*, on what I do with *my* character and how I engage with the world barring me breaking a rule. Just not a path we should go down for anything.

I think you're probably being earnest and honest in what you're saying and that your argument isn't bad faith, by the way, I just can't agree with it.

## Post 139920 by Elesion (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-07-16T16:11:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139920#p139920 | page 15 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Ardaran Feralsoul wrote: Wed Jul 16, 2025 3:15 pm**
> A silly jab aside, this doesn't look like any adventurer or mercenary I can think of in the game. Certainly doesn't look like any night elf druid either, definitely not part of the Cenarion Circle at that. If this is what the rest of the World of Warcraft sees from the eyes of NPCs, I can get why Fandral Staghelm hates all of us whenever we do a quest for him and tells us to, mostly politely, drop dead at the end of each of them. And also pretty strongly passes the check of "adventuring gear" considering no small body golden dragon nameplate boys are basically wearing Poor quality items and they seem to beat down pretty well (throw back to my post about this a couple pages ago).

I think it looks like adventuring gear because a mercenary adventuring into a molten hellscape to fight fire elementals would probably strive to cover up most of their body. No exposed skin and certainly no flowing hair, so the hood seems perfectly fitting. Also, you would wear whatever gets the job done and would protect you best. Adventuring is about killing the badies and surviving to tell the tale, so... a cohesive and fashionable look is last on your mind. And lastly, your gear tells a tale of your past adventures. When you sit around a campfire and someone admires your pants, you have a story to tell them. That kind of interaction doesn't happen when you transmog every single piece of loot immediately into a pre-raid outfit you somehow anchored your character to.

I have nothing against the transmog system. I use it extensively. But there is no lore/RP reason to insist on it imo. Both versions have their charm. Just because your gear might look like an ugly hodgepodge doesn't remotely mean it's RP-unfriendly or non-immersive.

## Post 139921 by Ardaran Feralsoul — 2025-07-16T16:28:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139921#p139921 | page 15 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Elesion wrote: Wed Jul 16, 2025 4:11 pm**
> I think it looks like adventuring gear because an mercenary adventuring into a molten hellscape to fight fire elementals would probably strive to cover up most of their body. No exposed skin and certainly no flowing hair, so the hood seems perfectly fitting. Also, you would wear whatever gets the job done and would protect you best. Adventuring is about killing the badies and surviving to tell the tale, so... a cohesive and fashionable look is last on your mind. And lastly, your gear tells a tale of your past adventures. When you it around a campfire and someone admires your pants, you have a story to tell them.
>
>  I have nothing against the transmog system. I use it extensively. But there is no lore/RP reason to insist on it imo. Both versions have their charm. Just because your gear might look like an ugly hodgepodge doesn't remotely mean it's RP-unfriendly or non-immersive.

Again I don't agree. We see plenty of characters in Azeroth who aren't wearing what we think would be "ideal" armor for their situation, mostly because they have spells and magical armor. This doesn't make internal sense at all from what the game shows in the world outside. *Plenty if not most all* other characters that aren't players. Even orc grunts who are the frontline fighters of the entire Horde have at least half their body exposed. Why? Orcs are just that tough and that's how their faction looks. Its ludonarrative *resonance*, counterpart to the more famous dissonance. The game tells us we don't need that kind of logic. Otherwise everyone would wear magic full plate, even Thrall, the one shaman who can wear plate apparently, has some straight cut off sleeves going on. He's not even wearing anything under his plate armor! Straight green skin to steel! Madness!

Anyway, you don't see druids layering themselves up in armor, not in Moonglade, not in Darkshore, not in Ashenvale or Feralas, certainly not in Darnassus, not even in Silithus where there's a literal war against the *Silithid* is taking place or hell, Hyjal for that matter where everything is dangerous. It doesn't make sense from the thematic and world angle the game presents itself with. I mean, clearly that was intended too given the opening cinematic druid is lucky her armor covers anything.

This is also just rolling with the assumption my character actual goes to Molten Core. Like someone said literal pages ago, no one in RP plays like that. No one honestly runs around and says they're the one who slew Majordomo Executus and summoned the Firelord (or more meme-y, defeated the lich, Kel'thuzad).

It makes a lot of sense from a lore and roleplaying reason to insist on it. I do think the old clown suits have charm but not here. I put up with that on real servers and lugged around a Manual Crowd Pummeler and Wolfshead Helm with my snazzy Cadaverous Armor, Belt of Preserved Heads, and a bunch of other cringe inducing nonsense while other people got to roll around in T1 or T2 (or lets be real, Bloodvine mostly).

Transmog is a distinct part of Turtle WoW, hands down, and anything negating that at all is making a pretty strong case for eroding a fundamental aspect of it (and just the theme of the game as it was originally made).

Very much yes. Transmog is great for collectors and for people who love to spend time on making great-looking sets, but for me, seeing the strange mix-and-match sets that people wear is part of Classic's charm. So yeah, I'd be very happy if I could toggle it off.

> >
> >  No, Vanilla is NOT unlike those games. In vanilla you can see if a Warrior is using Hakkari Warblades or R14 swords at a glance and know it is true. You can see if a Shaman is wielding the axe off Emperor Thaurissan or Dark Edge of Insanity and know that what you see is true.
> >
> >  Your opinion on what "proper" pvp is is inconsiquential when a fresh 60 doing AVs picks what he thinks is a decent chance at an even 1v1 and he gets literally 1 shot by a human paladin wearing the Robes of the Exalted and Demonshear when in reality he is wearing Plated Abomination Ribcage and the Corrupted Ashbringer
>
>   This is quite the constructed scenario. If you're seeking out 1v1s in AV then what are you even doing? Especially if you're looking only for people you can bully you can be sure that ashbringer wielding paladin is gonna do same, and will quickly see that he can bully you and charge your ass regardless. This thing goes both ways, you can't just ask everyone to line up politely so you can check which person you can destroy with ease. Actually, when you think about it, given your constructed scenario that paladin would quickly be able to tell that he could absolutely wreck that new lvl 60 - in a way, transmogging protects them from that. In reality of course he'd check your health bar etc much as everyone does in pvp anyways.
>
>  Also what paladin is gonna tmog Ashbringer

I am using the AV example because most people who play AV aren't actually pvpers. They are farming rep for the pve rewards. People like to seek out 1v1's because they want to have fun while theyre farming, which I know is a foreign concept on this server, but I assure you most people play games to feel good.

The scenario is one sided because the fresh 60 who has no fashion tokens and no bitches is giga-gapped not only in raw stats but also in the information available to them because the Naxx geared Paladin has access to the transmog disinformation and the fresh player does not.

"ask everyone to line up politely" Is exactly what every single no voter has been suggesting with "just inspect them" like every single pvp scenario starts with "X would like to duel you". The point of hiding transmogs in pvp would be so you can GLANCE at someone and see how strong they are because this is VANILLA and having a HIGH HEALTH BAR doesn't necessarily mean you are strong it just means you have a HIGH HEALTH BAR. This isnt retail where every piece of gear has:

Primary Stat 1
Stamina
Secondary Stat 1
Secondary Stat 2
Useless passive

## Post 139949 by Calibaar — 2025-07-16T18:53:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=139949#p139949 | page 15 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

The only case I see against a tmog toggle is the fact that people who have donated money towards the project might feel slighted. But a donation is a donation, not a purchase.

Also, I've modded my client quite a bit, so my representation of the game is quite literally different from how others see the game. I have altered textures, darker nights, different water. And none of that affects the enjoyment of the game for others, but it does change the enjoyment of the game for me.

So, I see this toggle as the same thing. I could change every item in the game on my client to look like a pony and basically destroy everyone's tmogs, and it wouldn't change their enjoyment in the slightest. I could even release that mod so that others can mod their own clients, so that there are who knows how many people out there seeing you dressed in ponies and it still won't affect those who choose to keep the toggle on.

There are some things that simply shouldn't be put to a poll and this is one of them. Transmog visibility should be a personal choice, not something dictated by the majority. Everyone should have the freedom to decide what they want to see in their own game experience.

If this feature had been implemented quietly, most players probably wouldn’t give a damn. But now that there’s a poll leaning toward no, implementing it anyway would only stir up unnecessary controversy.

It's important to remember: players using transmog would still see themselves in their chosen appearance. So really, it wouldn't negatively impact anyone, it would just give players more control over their own visual experience imo.

## Post 140068 by Fundead — 2025-07-17T11:41:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140068#p140068 | page 16 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

**Btw I asked this question to chatgpt for fun without giving any pros or cons and too much information to remove bias from the question, here is the answer, that I somewhat agree with:**

There are transmogs on Turtle wow private server and the poll of maybe there should be option for those who want it to turn them off, so they can see everybody without the transmogs, if they are using it. What would be you option and reasoning for that being good or bad thing to implement?

ChatGPT said:
Adding an option to disable transmogrification visuals (transmogs) on Turtle WoW — so that players can see others in their original gear appearance — is an interesting proposal. Here’s a breakdown of the pros and cons, and ultimately my take on whether it's a good idea or not.

![✅](//cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/twitter/twemoji@latest/assets/svg/2705.svg) Pros (Arguments for allowing players to disable transmogs)
Immersion and Lore Consistency

Turtle WoW leans heavily on roleplay and immersion. Seeing a warrior in brightly colored clown gear might break the illusion for someone who wants the world to feel grounded and classic.

Some players want gear to reflect progression. Transmogs can obscure that, making new players look like veterans or vice versa.

Player Agency

Giving players the choice empowers them. A toggle doesn’t force anyone to play a certain way — it lets people decide how they want to experience the game world.

PvP Readability

In world PvP or battlegrounds, knowing what gear someone has can inform your strategy. Transmogs can mask a player's gear level or class identity (especially in silhouette-heavy cases like mages in plate-looking robes).

RP-Friendly Option

Roleplayers might want to “see the world as it really is,” gear-wise. This could aid serious RP scenarios where consistency in appearance matters.

![❌](//cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/twitter/twemoji@latest/assets/svg/274c.svg) Cons (Arguments against the option)
Technical Complexity

Implementing this toggle may not be trivial. It would likely require client-side handling and syncing with server data in a non-standard way — especially tricky on a vanilla WoW private server.

Splitting the Playerbase Visually

Two players standing next to each other might see completely different worlds. One sees a knight in epic black plate, the other sees a clown in mismatched greens. This divergence could cause some confusion or reduce the shared experience.

Undermining Transmog Appeal

Transmog is partially about self-expression. If others can choose not to see your carefully curated appearance, it could lessen its value for some players.

Slippery Slope of Customization

Adding toggles for everything (transmog, mounts, spell effects, etc.) might bloat the client or cause inconsistency in the game world. A balance must be struck between customization and cohesion.

![🧠](//cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/twitter/twemoji@latest/assets/svg/1f9e0.svg) My Conclusion: Worth Considering, but Needs a Careful Approach
![✅](//cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/twitter/twemoji@latest/assets/svg/2705.svg) Yes, adding an optional toggle to disable viewing transmogs could be a good idea, especially for roleplayers or immersion-focused players. However, it must be implemented carefully to avoid technical instability and visual confusion.

If Turtle WoW’s development team can handle it cleanly (e.g., via a checkbox in the settings that forces all models to appear in their real gear, only for the local client), it could be a win-win feature. The key is that it should be optional, client-side, and lightweight.

## Post 140100 by Geeveeohh — 2025-07-17T16:27:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140100#p140100 | page 16 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I've posted several times but as I keep reading maybe a different clarification could aid?

 **Character Appearance Is Roleplay**
In an RP environment, a character’s visual design is not just cosmetic, it’s part of their identity, their story, and how they communicate who they are in the world.

Whether someone is portraying a wandering ranger in weathered leathers, a knight in ceremonial silverplate, or a mage in arcane-imbued robes, their appearance is intentional and part of how others perceive and interact with them.

A toggle that disables transmogrification silently strips away that intention. Without consent, another player’s view of your character becomes something unrecognizable, which can break immersion, undermine character arcs, and interrupt the shared narrative space we all contribute to.

 **Donor Investment Deserves Respect**
It’s also important to remember that transmog is a donor-unlocked feature. Many players have supported the server financially and invested time and creativity into designing their look.

A toggle that lets others opt out of seeing that investment removes the primary social value of the feature: being seen and understood as you intended. This isn’t about stats or power, it’s about identity. And being told “you can still enjoy it, even if others don’t see it” misses the point entirely in an RP world where presence matters.

 **Final Thoughts**
While customization is a good thing, and I understand that some prefer to see “real” gear for their own immersion or PvP tracking, a blanket toggle undermines the very nature of RP. It affects the way others experience you, without your knowledge or agreement.

If a solution is sought, perhaps it could be:

Voluntary, where you can choose to let your appearance be overridden, but not by default. Allow a PLAYER the option to turn it off for others, but not give others that control of another player. So rather than remove from tmog, add a feature to it that it can be 'suppressed' maybe by that individual player.

But a system that changes how others perceive your character without consent isn’t just a toggle. It’s a break in the shared storytelling contract that makes this community so rich.

Thanks for reading.

## Post 140157 by Akos1896 (Grandmaster of Forum PvP) — 2025-07-17T21:00:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140157#p140157 | page 17 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Gave Chatgpt the prompth and asked it to give a really sarcastic opinion.
Not chiming in the debate with this, just for fun.

Let’s get into the pros and cons of this earth-shattering decision.

Pros:
Fashion Anarchy, but Only If You Want It
Now you can choose to see that gnome tank in a pink dress and bunny ears… or not. Your immersion, your rules! Because what’s more Vanilla+ than toggling other people’s sense of style out of existence?

Keep the “Vanilla Experience”™ Sacred (For Yourself)
Miss the days when everyone looked like mismatched trash until they hit BWL? Well, now you can literally pretend it’s 2006, even if your friends are prancing around in their curated clown suits.

Ultimate Control
Next step: toggling out world buffs, pvp flags, and players you don’t like. The power is yours!

Cons:
Shared World, Solo Vision
MMOs are about shared experiences, but why stop there? Let’s all play in our own individual Truman Shows. Maybe next patch, we get a slider for how much fun everyone else is allowed to have.

That Guy in the Raid
Get ready for that one person who insists no one else transmogs “because it ruins their immersion,” but is totally fine using the switch to ignore reality.

Technical Chaos
What could possibly go wrong? Surely, adding a toggle that rewrites everyone’s appearances client-side will never, ever cause bugs, glitches, or those fun moments where someone shows up as a naked night elf. Never.

Diminishing Transmog Flex
You farmed those tokens, slayed those bosses, and coordinated that perfect look. But for half the players, you’ll still just be “random paladin #15 in banana shoulders.” Hope your self-esteem is raid-ready.

My Verdict: Take a Side!
Let’s be honest. If you’re playing Turtle Wow in 2025, you either:

Adore Vanilla and want things exactly as they were (except, of course, for all the quality of life changes, extra races, mounts, and, uh, transmog), or
Love the chaos and color that comes from seeing a tauren warrior in a tuxedo with a chef’s hat.

So, do we really need a switch? Sure, add it. Why not! If people want to turn off transmogs, let them. While we’re at it, give them a toggle for “disable fun,” “block dance emotes,” and “auto-mute anyone who types ‘LF2M for SM Arm.’” Everyone gets a toggle! Oprah-style.
But let’s not pretend this will make everyone happy, or that it “solves” anything. MMOs are about seeing and being seen. If you don’t want to see other people’s choices, maybe, just maybe, the problem isn’t with the transmogs.

Final Answer:
Add the switch. Let people have their precious toggle. But don’t be surprised when the most popular add-on is “Disable Toggle Enjoyers.” Because in the end, the only true Vanilla+ experience… is arguing about immersion on Discord.

## Post 140172 by Ardaran Feralsoul — 2025-07-17T22:14:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140172#p140172 | page 17 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Downwithopp wrote: Thu Jul 17, 2025 10:01 pm**
> You keep using words that mean something other than what you are trying to say.  Not sure if you are trying to convince yourself or others as well.

That's the fun part - I'm not.

I don't need to convince myself or anyone else. Anyone who's remotely grounded in reality gets how silly an argument that is. I know people won't read this, and won't bother reading the whole thread by the numbers where I went over this already, but here's a real question.

Are you a narcissist for dressing the way you do and going outside every day and other people just having to deal with it? Let's walk this back a bit more, lets gamify it. Are you a narcissist for dressing your character the way you do and logging in to run around and other people just having to deal with it?

If that parallel isn't more than 100% clear, I don't think I or anyone else can help you.

It's not narcissism and its not narcissistic of *anyone* to go by those standards. Its not a) self important (literally doesn't meet the criteria of "superior to others" and "special treatment"), b) a need for validation (obviously the skimpy bunny girls and the tauren in dresses don't match this), c) a lack of empathy (the people most ardently defending transmog pretty by and large seem to do it for a cohesive game world and immersion in what is WoW so, probably not a lack of empathy). *At absolute best*, it *can* be exploitative (PvPers everywhere whinge) and or could be demanding of admiration (which is... the exact same argument for a fair number of "yes" voters because they want people to have to acknowledge their Uber T3 too as much as transmoggers want to be seen with their custom sets.)

I think by pretty broad definitions "yes" getting to have special self importance (like, you know, getting *special treatment*, the option) to turn off other people's appearances without their consent, a need for validation so strong it takes a poll and groupthink to even stand a chance, and also doesn't seem to care about how other people feel all that much is... maybe... narcissism.

But hey, keep doing you. If you like that word and you want to use it another way, I can't stop you.
