## Post 140212 by Altimateturtle — 2025-07-18T03:32:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140212#p140212 | page 17 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Fundead wrote: Wed Jul 16, 2025 10:23 pm**
> > **Braxxis wrote: Wed Jul 16, 2025 9:58 pm**
> > If you're going to make it an option for others to disable how your tmog appears to them, then it better also come along with free tmog. Because fashion coins cost 25 gold per, and tmogs are item, not slot-specific. That's a lot of commitment and investment just to sink into an endgame hobby that people can toggle off on a whim.
>
>   No. its a free choice to tmog or not. It's like saying everybody needs to play pve because you invested in pve gear. Its your choice. I can toggle off pve  and just don't do it. And people who enjoy that aspect of the game can do it. Same as people who enjoy just leveling, questing, pve, pvp or doing nothing, do what you want with others who also enjoy it or alone if you like. Stop thinking about yourself only for a second or feel that important, as you are not, nobody is, that is the point. Lets everybody enjoy the game we like to play how we like to play it.
>
>  To the post above - yes, MMORPG is about showing your gear not faking it (for me mmorpg is really about everything else except showing my gear really), that's why I am against tmogs, they ruin the RP for me. Just dressing up for RP events or whateva does not.

It may be a free choice for a person to do transmogs,  but it is definitely NOT A FREE THING TO DO.  And there is a difference between those two.  While anyone can choose to transmog or not transmog,  if one does do transmog there is a cost to doing it.  Whether the cost is time from farming fashion coins, whether it is actual money from the donation shop for the coins, or whether the cost is in game gold that you paid to buy the quest items and turn in the quest to earn the fashion coins,  every way you slice it,  it cost something.  So therefore transmog has a VALUE to it, represented by whatever people were willing to do to obtain the coins.  It is a virtual value on a game, not a physical thing, but it still has alot of meaning to a whole bunch of players who have put that effort in to obtain those fashion coins.   What is transmog?  Transmog means that I didn't like the way that my gear looked and I wanted to change the appearance of it-  whether I was bored of it or whether I wanted to keep the stats of that piece of gear while making it look more appealing than it does on its own.  So I am paying fashion coins for the fashionista to make me look cool- to look the way I want in front of other players,  because other people will see me and have opinions of me- even from just a first impression.  That's the service.  In business, people pay money, they get goods or services in exchange.  My character looking better on the server is the service, that I am paying for by doing transmog, and spending coins.  Plain and simple.  Now what if there is a toggle that is available for the whole server to potentially turn off the transmog.  Not everyone will use it, but there are a handful of people who will turn it off.  So I am still paying the fashion coins - still paying for this, but now the conditions of what I am buying have changed.  The conditions of the service have changed.  Before I pay, and I look good across the server, now the deal has been altered, and the new terms are, I pay, and I look good to some people across the server.  Suddenly its different because those two things are not the same thing.  The meaning it had before is now partially lost.  Because I am still paying, but now, not everyone will see me wearing what I paid for.  People can now ignore the appearance that I spent so much time earning. And people can see my mix matched clown get up.  So by have this toggle in place, even only as a partial thing that only some people will use,  you have changed the terms of the deal, made the meaning different of what we are buying, and thus DEVALUED the service that we were paying for.  Because we would still be paying for the same thing.  We have spent the amount already, but now, WE ARE GETTING LESS FOR OUR MONEY .  That friends, is what you call, getting ripped off.  So all the yes voters who think this isn't harming anything,  you are harming the definition of what transmogging means to the players on this server.  And guess what, we are not okay with that.  We have not spent so much effort on transmog over the years, just for you to come in and ruin it.  Why should we have people seeing us in mix matched gear when we have spent hours getting fashion coins to avoid that.  I'm not against them implementing a true gear check in pvp or battlegrounds to make things better for you, but leave our transmogs alone.  Because if you touch this, then transmog loses its meaning to us, and you are spitting on something that has been established for a long time here.  This is not your perfect nostalgia based classic server.  this is turtle wow. it is a custom server.  Just wait til unreal engine hits.  This also effects the in game economy for the auction house.  There are times that I have bought a piece of gear , just for the look of it, so I could transmog it onto something else.  That's money I gave to other players in game and bought their auctions.  If that kind of thing stopped happening, then it changes the flow of money on the server.

## Post 140219 by Avendor — 2025-07-18T05:17:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140219#p140219 | page 18 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

**Transmog Question**
Few people like or have the time to read a 17-page long forum topic and then some, especially when a fair chunk is people yelling at each other or being bad faith. I decided to try and gather the arguments I see together in hopes of centralizing what both sides are arguing for. This took me a few hours, in part because I'm a slow reader, so I hope y'all are grateful!

Full disclosure that I'm on the naysaying side, you can see a post for why below.
> **Avendor wrote: Tue Jul 15, 2025 6:25 am**
> <Post explaining Toggle Function's effect upon an RPer's mind>

That said, I will try to be as objective as I can be for this particular post, and maybe add more opinions in a second post if I feel like it. Just don't be surprised if I seem to have a better grasp on one side's arguments than the other; take it with my apologies and correct me if I got something wrong or missed a point that should've gone on the list.

 **Yaysayers:**
 - Vanilla Immersion
Some players are here because they want the Classic experience. Some new additions like edited zones are probably not a contradiction unless they want pure Vanilla. There is an element of player identity here too as if you see the true models of any given gear piece, those gear pieces tell a story of what you've been up to, the luck you've had and the work you've done. By showing the true models of those pieces you've collected, you're showcasing your achivements in the game.

 - PvP
It leads to an issue in PvP where you can't trust your eyes and properly estimate the strength of your enemies. One might say "just look at HP/MP" or "just inspect bro", but inspecting is -not- viable in PvP situations, and while HP/MP should be default, PvP can be very "on-the-second", especially if you get ambushed. Being able to trust visual input does have an impact. Furthermore, being able to tell who wears what gear is a good way of picking out what kind of a <Class> someone is. White robes and staff? Probably Holy Priest. Big hammer and some pieces of Rogue Dungeon sets? Probably a Feral Druid, etc.

 - More Options
Related to Vanilla Immersion, by having a toggle, people can choose whether they prefer to see transmogs or if they fancy more traditional Classic player aesthetics. People advocating this one argue that those who want toggles can still have them, while those who don't can go without and it won't affect gameplay. If nothing else, it's true from a PvE perspective, I don't think anyone can't argue against that.

 - Slutmogs & Bunny Ears
Some people have really provocative looks to their characters- usually female -and linger about in said aesthetic all day. That and silly items like bunny ears take away from the Warcraft aesthetics we all presumably want from the game, RPers and PvPers alike. While we can't do anything about people who just unequip everything or saved a silly holiday equipment, it would at least reduce the amount of plate bikini and bunny ears we see around Stormwind in particular.

 - Nobody Cares What You Look Like
A frequent argument I hear, and I don't know how to be "objective" on this one. At risk of personal opinion:
I find it a poor one, because if nobody cares then they have no horse in the race one way or the other and therefore would abstain, but I wanted to throw it out there as something I've seen. Besides, I care what I look like. I care about the level of control I have to present myself the way I wanna. Y'know? If nobody else cares, maybe the one who does care should take priority? Is a thought. I exaggerate a little bit there because I don't actually think they don't care. Not least cuz of the point made above this one. I'll touch upon this point once or twice again as I go.

 - There Are Far Worse Offenders Like Rainbow Cloud Mounts Out There.
Some have argued that since there are a lot of immersion-breaking things out there, like the aforementioned mounts, or dragonkin illusions etc etc, the idea of a toggle shouldn't be so bad from a roleplaying perspective.

 **Naysayers:**
 - Roleplay Immersion.
It's hard to escape the "this won't affect you!" argument, even when I tried to deep-dive into why it does. I will address that again down below. But fact is, appearances are -extremely- important for roleplayers, speaking as one myself. It's in fact the second-most important aspect next to your ability to write and play out a lore-fitting role for your character. By having Transmogs, we can filter out the clowngear we pick up from raids and select our preferred look that actually matches our character and the world better.

If something is too flashy (like Benediction), we can turn it into something less flashy (like Redemption), or alter red gear to be blue instead to match Alliance aesthetics. This is big for High Elven characters in particular since wearing red is a bit like a political statement lorewise. With a good chance that our aesthetics are rendered moot, the result is the whole dysphoria and wall of separation between anyone who sees tmogs and anyone who doesn't, it's hard to immerse when you feel unsure who is seeing what. WoW is a social game too, and for the RP front that goes triple.

 - It Creates A Barrier Between Us
Having some people see one thing and other people see another means we're nolonger living in the same world, we're undermining the communal aspect of the game. Yaysayers will argue "well you'll never know anyway", which is probably true, but all the more reason for why I'm gonna struggle to get into the world I'm being sold. Others will argue "well it doesn't change the gameplay in any way", which is true. The -PvE gameplay- won't change.
But my -RP immersion- will. Will a toggle kill RP? Probably not. Will it punch it in the gut? I think so, because I know it discourages me specifically from wanting to engage in it when a dysphoric voice in my head is whispering to me "you might be a clown on their screen" in the back of my mind. If you read this and think "well mods though!" you should scroll down to the honorary mentions section.

 - WYDM I'm Wearing Clowngear?
A lot of gear pieces in Classic look awful, especially when mixed with items that have no business being near each other. To many it's important to look good as well as perform, and that's where transmogs shine. To have the ability to toggle it off means you'll be seen in clowngear by a fair portion of the playerbase which just feels bad to know it's a factor. Any MMO that isn't effectively a singleplayer game in disguise is gonna have the world and your fellow players matter, and part of that is making your own impression upon it. The first and most overt step of that is choosing your own aesthetic look. Overriding that and what I look like will matter as much as if you'd have me look like a skeleton the entire game.

 - My Character, My Choice
This is very related to "More Options" because it's effectively the same argument from the opposite direction. Is it your choice to not see what I present, or my choice to present it in the first place? Where does my game end and your game begin and vice versa?

The pro-choice argue it doesn't affect others if you enable or disable, but it does in fact remove the value of the transmog, and it makes having transmogs not feel impactful because now there's lord only know how many people out there who see your character as something else, and your ability to present yourself to the world around you is significantly reduced.

At risk of adding personal opinion to this post; to me it reminds me of people who'd say "you can be gay, but be gay where I don't see it". It also plays into player identity with how one chooses to present oneself; what sort of Paladin do you want to be seen as? Are you a Warrior who prefers swords or axes? Things of that nature.

 - It Cheapens the Entire Point of Tmogs
I also call this the "Collector's Mindset". Nobody can in good faith argue that the tmogs will be as impactful as they are now if we give people the option to just not see it. Well why the hell am I gathering all those pieces and transmog options then? Just to see it to myself? The point of the MMO is that I'm not playing by myself. Knowing that I'm effectively pushed into a corner is a bad vibe and completely kills the desire to hunt for gear. To say nothing for how little the Fashion Coins will suddenly be worth.

 - People Have Paid RL Money For This
I don't know how many people have done this, but it's true, you can buy Fashion Coins for real life money, and some people definitely have. This is retroactively a slap in the face for them and takes away value from their purchase which is bad practice. I'm not sure how to resolve that one, other than by some sort of compensation. Someone argued that a donation is not a purchase, but don't be fooled by the word "donation" being used. You pay money, you get in-game goods. That's a purchase, not a donation, even if it might be motivated by a desire to assist the server by covering for its expenses.

 - Less People Will Donate RL Money
It's related to the above points, but odds are that if you make the transmogs hollow, then you can't use it as a salespitch to encourage people to pay money to acquire them, and in the process supporting the server. Or at worst, they might do it not realizing until after the fact that their purchase can just be invalidated by someone and therefore get pissed off and refuse to buy anything else. This does, of course, not affect those who give money with express intent to aid the server and only see the donor rewards as a bonus for the effort.

 - Bag Space
It's also worth mentioning that I don't actually have many of the gear pieces that are saved in the tmog window since there was never a need to actually save them, so I can't simply carry them with me and wear them whenever. I could go out and reacquire them, but beyond the extra work some pieces I have are tier sets which will be hell to acquire when there are people who need it for stats. Plus, ya know. Bag space. I struggle with that already as is without needing to carry three bags of gear with me for a given occasion.

 - Nordanaar is a RP-PvE Realm, Tel'Abim is a PvP Realm
It's true Nordanaar leans more on PvE than RP these days, but it has never appealed directly to PvP players as a target audience. Whereas Tel'Abim has from the getgo. The crux of this argument is that RPers and PvEers should get preferential treatment over the PvPers since this is a server that's supposed to cater to them first and foremost, whereas PvPers should get the preferential treatment on Tel'Abim.

 -It Opens the Door to Gear Elitism
The argument is that by showing true gear, some people might judge your abilities based on the gear you're wearing. I don't buy this one myself, inspections would immediately shut it down regardless of what you look like, and I don't think most people are that elitist on average, but I wanted to present all the arguments I see which don't strike me as bad faith.

 **Compromise:**
 - Nordanaar is a RP-PvE Realm, Tel'Abim is a PvP Realm Part 2
Some argue RPers are a minority on Nordanaar, which is true, but so are PvPers, and it doesn't change what the server is marketed as, so the counter-argument rings a bit hollow. Does make for a good setup to a 50/50 compromise where Tel'Abim gets the change and Nordanaar doesn't though.

 - No Tmogs in Battlegrounds & Arenas (or those flagged PvP)
If its a viable approach from a coding perspective, having Tmogs temporarily disabled in Battlegrounds and Arenas sound perfectly fair and valid. Those are removed instances and not part of the wider world, it's a place you go in specifically to play the game in, not to roleplay, and it'll let PvPers gain that visual output they seek when in their element. I don't think anyone would be unhappy with that. Removing Tmogs when flagged in general is a more tricky one, because now you -are- out in the world, and some people are still working on their warmode leveling no doubt, but at least there'd be a light at the end of the tunnel for those who care.

 - Limit Transmogs To Types
Which is to say plate armor can only be xmogged into different plate, swords only to different swords etc. No sword to axe, no mail to cloth etc. This way we can always be sure that someone is wearing a particular type of armor and calculate how squishy they might be or how hard/fast they might swing as a result. I wonder if this isn't the "make everybody equally upset" option myself, but it's worth mentioning.

 - Add a Toggle Functionality When Inspecting
So when you inspect someone's gear, would you like a button to remove their transmogs in that one window? Well, why not! It doesn't affect the world around us, it serves something of a gameplay purpose, and curious people can just choose to peek and see without actually removing anything from the one they're peeking at. There is a layer of removal between what you're looking at and what I'm presenting to the world.

 - Transmog Toggle Function For Joke Items
The idea being that items like brooms and bunny ears etc etc DO have a toggle function associated with them based on item class or something, but "normal" item pieces like Red Bandana Mask or so are not marked as a joke item, and therefore can't be toggled off. Not sure how viable it if from a programming standpoint, but it might just address the slutmog point without striking with a broad brush to fuck with everyone's tmogs regardless of how provacative it is or isn't.

 - Make Those Who Disable Tmogs Visible
One of the concerns I've mentioned is we won't know who sees the tmogged set I've made for myself, and therefore that voice of doubt and hesitation in the back of my head is persistent. This approach would make those who turn the option off visible somehow, maybe with a title like the Hardcore'ers have ("Seer"?), an icon by their name, by appearing on the map somehow, or maybe just with a permanent buff to be seen when clicked. Either way, that does make it clear who is seeing what. Still awkward AF if someone has that in an RP situation for whatever reason, but dems the cake. It still helps overall.

 - Tmog Ignore List
A compromise where the idea is that you can turn off tmogs for select people rather than just everyone. I don't know how viable this is from a coding perspective, but it does address the slutmog issue if nothing else.

 - A Toggle To Allow
Instead of a toggle to just undo all transmogs, the idea proposed was a toggle you yourself check in to let others undo your transmogs. It's a fair one if nothing else, though at risk of personal opinion, I doubt how utilized it would be or how happy anybody would get from it.

 **Honorary Mention:**
 - U NAYSAYERS R JUST NARCCISIST/BOTS/SOCKACCOUNTS
k. Any good faith Yaysayers out there to engage with? Oh hello arguments in the Yaysayer category, nice to see you!

 - People Use Mods Anyway, Same Thing!
This one strikes me as bad faith, so I didn't wanna include it under the Yaysayer banner. But I wanted to address the actual argument, so here goes:

We can't do anything about people who use mods for one, and for another it's a matter of accessibility. Sorta like guns; sure you might be able to lay your hands on one via the black market or so and voila. But tell me which country is easier to get a gun in: United States or Japan? Compare and contrast their numbers of gun owners.

By a similar token, maybe someone does have a mod that changes everyone on their screen into a naked female Draenei, but how much worse and more common does it get if TWoW decided to make that a standard toggle feature in the interface menu? You see the issue right?

 - It's Been Around For 14+ Years!
True, though it's not a very strong argument as it doesn't mean any Yaysayer necessarily like the fact, not does it invalidate why they'd speak up so strongly now when they have a chance at affecting the outcome. I wanted to mention that.

 - Tmogs May or May Not Be Fine, But Illusions Man!
People want the option to turn off illusions too, either for immersion purposes, PvP balance or both. They hit on very similar vibes, and they're both connected to the question of IRL money spent to acquire them. I don't disagree they fuck with my roleplay immersion, but I feel it's more an extension of the entire question than a direct addressing of it one way or the other.

 - TWoW Is Just Following Epoch's Lead
I guess? Anyway moving on.

 - A Toggle Option Doesn't Remove the Feature!
Not outright, and no one is saying that it does, but it does hollow it out and renders it cheap and pointless. See the Naysayer category for further details.

 - Remove Transmogs, Add Outfitter!
I saw someone suggest this, and I'm frankly not entirely sure what the difference is. Do you keep the gear in your bags or in some sort of bank-esque tab you can switch in and out of like with dual spec? I donno, but I wanted to leave it in for people to chew on as I can see the theoretical potential.

 - Transmogging Can Be Used As A Disguise In PvP!
It's true. Warfare is often built upon deception, or ability to hide your means from the other side. It opens up doors for PvP strategy by cleverly disguising what you have. Whether or not this is a good thing here is a question I think you will have to answer, especially if you're a PvPer, which I'm not.

 - Who Cares? Work On Something Else!
I sort of can't disagree I think resources should go to other matters?   happy_turtle_head
But I'm very "I support the notion, I know nothing of the specifics" about it, so here we are. Maybe more efforts to speed up patch release so I can finally check out Northwind and Dun Kithas? Would be nice~

 - What About Naked People?
I don't have a strong opinion myself, but if the devs wanna remove the completely nude pieces that remove underwear and leave your character a barbie, I don't oppose. It has no roleplay value (if you really need to get naked, you can unequip everything down to your underwear and achieve the same result), and it has no aesthetic value. Even from a horny perspective, it's a barbie. You'd be better off looking for porn on the internet. But I can also just move along.

 - The Poll Could've Used More Nuanced Options
Like "BGs Only" or the likes. It's a fair point, I think, but we work with what we have.

 - Toggle Could Lead To Harassment of RPers!
While an RPer on the Naysaying side, I frankly don't see this one at all. Trolls out to ruin things don't need it and can't effectively weaponize it to mess with us or try to ruin our events. And if it does happen? That's what the Report function exists for. Be sure to take screenshots.

 - Transmogs Are Digital Art
Yes, but I also think this argument can be made in the opposite direction. As has been mentioned, standard Classic gear- while often mismatched aesthetically -can create some degree of storytelling in and of itself by showcasing where the player has been and who they've spanked over the course of their journey playing the game. That or they bought it off the Auction House. Either or.

 - And Lastly
This fucking meme! I love it and everybody deserve to see it.  satisfied_turtle_head

## Post 140229 by Bootlegmozart1 — 2025-07-18T06:03:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140229#p140229 | page 18 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I'm gonna go with No because it's more immersive that way. I'd venture to say people put more time into tmogs than they do anything else, especially on Nordnaar. Nudemogs and mememogs make the game better. People who disagree with that are either prudes or fun-police.

If anything, I'd suggest the toggle option for the PvP server instead of the RP server. I think the toggle should go in the inspect window instead of having it be an option elsewhere.

Granted, it'd be nice to have some form of, "I can see the armor you have equipped at a glance to see if I can beat you in a 1v1", however I don't think it'd do much other than upset the RPers. I rarely have time to observe someone's equipment before committing to a battle anyway, and I find it to be weenie behavior to complain about not knowing other people's gear on sight. Yeah, it sucks to make a bad call and die, but that's part of the game. PvP is optional. The PvP dedicated server is Right There.

So yeah, put the toggle on the PvP realm, but not the RP realm.

## Post 140267 by Dannyp92 (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-07-18T09:32:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140267#p140267 | page 18 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

The devs should look into how the opinions on this subject differs between Nordanaar and Tel Abim. They're quite different communities at this point, with different needs. Thus, sweeping changes across both servers are not a good idea. Because Nordanaar is much bigger, they will dominate every poll. Polls should be directed separately for each community.

On another note, I struggle to understand how so many can be against this option. If it did exist, the vast majority of those that love transmog will want to see the transmog of other players, and because the majority of the server likes transmog to an extent, only a small fraction of the population would turn it off. The vast majority of RPers and transmog-enjoyers will still enjoy each other's transmog. How does it matter to you that 100 % of playerbase can see your transmog, vs. maybe 80 % of the playerbase? You will still be getting more than plenty attention for your chosen outfit.

In this case (as opposed to something like a level boost), giving others the freedom to opt out doesn’t diminish your experience, it simply respects theirs.

## Post 140271 by Ooshawnyoo — 2025-07-18T09:48:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140271#p140271 | page 18 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Avendor wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 5:17 am**
> **Transmog Question**
>  Few people like or have the time to read a 17-page long forum topic and then some, especially when a fair chunk is people yelling at each other or being bad faith. I decided to try and gather the arguments I see together in hopes of centralizing what both sides are arguing for. This took me a few hours, in part because I'm a slow reader, so I hope y'all are grateful!
>
>  Full disclosure that I'm on the naysaying side, you can see a post for why below.
> > **Avendor wrote: Tue Jul 15, 2025 6:25 am**
> > <Post explaining Toggle Function's effect upon an RPer's mind>
>
>  That said, I will try to be as objective as I can be for this particular post, and maybe add more opinions in a second post if I feel like it. Just don't be surprised if I seem to have a better grasp on one side's arguments than the other; take it with my apologies and correct me if I got something wrong or missed a point that should've gone on the list.
>
>  **Yaysayers:**
>  - Vanilla Immersion
>  Some players are here because they want the Classic experience. Some new additions like edited zones are probably not a contradiction unless they want pure Vanilla. There is an element of player identity here too as if you see the true models of any given gear piece, those gear pieces tell a story of what you've been up to, the luck you've had and the work you've done. By showing the true models of those pieces you've collected, you're showcasing your achivements in the game.
>
>  - PvP
>  It leads to an issue in PvP where you can't trust your eyes and properly estimate the strength of your enemies. One might say "just look at HP/MP" or "just inspect bro", but inspecting is -not- viable in PvP situations, and while HP/MP should be default, PvP can be very "on-the-second", especially if you get ambushed. Being able to trust visual input does have an impact. Furthermore, being able to tell who wears what gear is a good way of picking out what kind of a <Class> someone is. White robes and staff? Probably Holy Priest. Big hammer and some pieces of Rogue Dungeon sets? Probably a Feral Druid, etc.
>
>  - More Options
>  Related to Vanilla Immersion, by having a toggle, people can choose whether they prefer to see transmogs or if they fancy more traditional Classic player aesthetics. People advocating this one argue that those who want toggles can still have them, while those who don't can go without and it won't affect gameplay. If nothing else, it's true from a PvE perspective, I don't think anyone can't argue against that.
>
>  - Slutmogs & Bunny Ears
>  Some people have really provocative looks to their characters- usually female -and linger about in said aesthetic all day. That and silly items like bunny ears take away from the Warcraft aesthetics we all presumably want from the game, RPers and PvPers alike. While we can't do anything about people who just unequip everything or saved a silly holiday equipment, it would at least reduce the amount of plate bikini and bunny ears we see around Stormwind in particular.
>
>  - Nobody Cares What You Look Like
>  A frequent argument I hear, and I don't know how to be "objective" on this one. At risk of personal opinion:
>  I find it a poor one, because if nobody cares then they have no horse in the race one way or the other and therefore would abstain, but I wanted to throw it out there as something I've seen. Besides, I care what I look like. I care about the level of control I have to present myself the way I wanna. Y'know? If nobody else cares, maybe the one who does care should take priority? Is a thought. I exaggerate a little bit there because I don't actually think they don't care. Not least cuz of the point made above this one. I'll touch upon this point once or twice again as I go.
>
>  - There Are Far Worse Offenders Like Rainbow Cloud Mounts Out There.
>  Some have argued that since there are a lot of immersion-breaking things out there, like the aforementioned mounts, or dragonkin illusions etc etc, the idea of a toggle shouldn't be so bad from a roleplaying perspective.
>
>  **Naysayers:**
>  - Roleplay Immersion.
>  It's hard to escape the "this won't affect you!" argument, even when I tried to deep-dive into why it does. I will address that again down below. But fact is, appearances are -extremely- important for roleplayers, speaking as one myself. It's in fact the second-most important aspect next to your ability to write and play out a lore-fitting role for your character. By having Transmogs, we can filter out the clowngear we pick up from raids and select our preferred look that actually matches our character and the world better.
>
>  If something is too flashy (like Benediction), we can turn it into something less flashy (like Redemption), or alter red gear to be blue instead to match Alliance aesthetics. This is big for High Elven characters in particular since wearing red is a bit like a political statement lorewise. With a good chance that our aesthetics are rendered moot, the result is the whole dysphoria and wall of separation between anyone who sees tmogs and anyone who doesn't, it's hard to immerse when you feel unsure who is seeing what. WoW is a social game too, and for the RP front that goes triple.
>
>  - It Creates A Barrier Between Us
>  Having some people see one thing and other people see another means we're nolonger living in the same world, we're undermining the communal aspect of the game. Yaysayers will argue "well you'll never know anyway", which is probably true, but all the more reason for why I'm gonna struggle to get into the world I'm being sold. Others will argue "well it doesn't change the gameplay in any way", which is true. The -PvE gameplay- won't change.
>  But my -RP immersion- will. Will a toggle kill RP? Probably not. Will it punch it in the gut? I think so, because I know it discourages me specifically from wanting to engage in it when a dysphoric voice in my head is whispering to me "you might be a clown on their screen" in the back of my mind. If you read this and think "well mods though!" you should scroll down to the honorary mentions section.
>
>  - WYDM I'm Wearing Clowngear?
>  A lot of gear pieces in Classic look awful, especially when mixed with items that have no business being near each other. To many it's important to look good as well as perform, and that's where transmogs shine. To have the ability to toggle it off means you'll be seen in clowngear by a fair portion of the playerbase which just feels bad to know it's a factor. Any MMO that isn't effectively a singleplayer game in disguise is gonna have the world and your fellow players matter, and part of that is making your own impression upon it. The first and most overt step of that is choosing your own aesthetic look. Overriding that and what I look like will matter as much as if you'd have me look like a skeleton the entire game.
>
>  - My Character, My Choice
>  This is very related to "More Options" because it's effectively the same argument from the opposite direction. Is it your choice to not see what I present, or my choice to present it in the first place? Where does my game end and your game begin and vice versa?
>
>  The pro-choice argue it doesn't affect others if you enable or disable, but it does in fact remove the value of the transmog, and it makes having transmogs not feel impactful because now there's lord only know how many people out there who see your character as something else, and your ability to present yourself to the world around you is significantly reduced.
>
>  At risk of adding personal opinion to this post; to me it reminds me of people who'd say "you can be gay, but be gay where I don't see it". It also plays into player identity with how one chooses to present oneself; what sort of Paladin do you want to be seen as? Are you a Warrior who prefers swords or axes? Things of that nature.
>
>  - It Cheapens the Entire Point of Tmogs
>  I also call this the "Collector's Mindset". Nobody can in good faith argue that the tmogs will be as impactful as they are now if we give people the option to just not see it. Well why the hell am I gathering all those pieces and transmog options then? Just to see it to myself? The point of the MMO is that I'm not playing by myself. Knowing that I'm effectively pushed into a corner is a bad vibe and completely kills the desire to hunt for gear. To say nothing for how little the Fashion Coins will suddenly be worth.
>
>  - People Have Paid RL Money For This
>  I don't know how many people have done this, but it's true, you can buy Fashion Coins for real life money, and some people definitely have. This is retroactively a slap in the face for them and takes away value from their purchase which is bad practice. I'm not sure how to resolve that one, other than by some sort of compensation. Someone argued that a donation is not a purchase, but don't be fooled by the word "donation" being used. You pay money, you get in-game goods. That's a purchase, not a donation, even if it might be motivated by a desire to assist the server by covering for its expenses.
>
>  - Less People Will Donate RL Money
>  It's related to the above points, but odds are that if you make the transmogs hollow, then you can't use it as a salespitch to encourage people to pay money to acquire them, and in the process supporting the server. Or at worst, they might do it not realizing until after the fact that their purchase can just be invalidated by someone and therefore get pissed off and refuse to buy anything else. This does, of course, not affect those who give money with express intent to aid the server and only see the donor rewards as a bonus for the effort.
>
>  - Bag Space
>  It's also worth mentioning that I don't actually have many of the gear pieces that are saved in the tmog window since there was never a need to actually save them, so I can't simply carry them with me and wear them whenever. I could go out and reacquire them, but beyond the extra work some pieces I have are tier sets which will be hell to acquire when there are people who need it for stats. Plus, ya know. Bag space. I struggle with that already as is without needing to carry three bags of gear with me for a given occasion.
>
>  - Nordanaar is a RP-PvE Realm, Tel'Abim is a PvP Realm
>  It's true Nordanaar leans more on PvE than RP these days, but it has never appealed directly to PvP players as a target audience. Whereas Tel'Abim has from the getgo. The crux of this argument is that RPers and PvEers should get preferential treatment over the PvPers since this is a server that's supposed to cater to them first and foremost, whereas PvPers should get the preferential treatment on Tel'Abim.
>
>  -It Opens the Door to Gear Elitism
>  The argument is that by showing true gear, some people might judge your abilities based on the gear you're wearing. I don't buy this one myself, inspections would immediately shut it down regardless of what you look like, and I don't think most people are that elitist on average, but I wanted to present all the arguments I see which don't strike me as bad faith.
>
>  **Compromise:**
>  - Nordanaar is a RP-PvE Realm, Tel'Abim is a PvP Realm Part 2
>  Some argue RPers are a minority on Nordanaar, which is true, but so are PvPers, and it doesn't change what the server is marketed as, so the counter-argument rings a bit hollow. Does make for a good setup to a 50/50 compromise where Tel'Abim gets the change and Nordanaar doesn't though.
>
>  - No Tmogs in Battlegrounds & Arenas (or those flagged PvP)
>  If its a viable approach from a coding perspective, having Tmogs temporarily disabled in Battlegrounds and Arenas sound perfectly fair and valid. Those are removed instances and not part of the wider world, it's a place you go in specifically to play the game in, not to roleplay, and it'll let PvPers gain that visual output they seek when in their element. I don't think anyone would be unhappy with that. Removing Tmogs when flagged in general is a more tricky one, because now you -are- out in the world, and some people are still working on their warmode leveling no doubt, but at least there'd be a light at the end of the tunnel for those who care.
>
>  - Limit Transmogs To Types
>  Which is to say plate armor can only be xmogged into different plate, swords only to different swords etc. No sword to axe, no mail to cloth etc. This way we can always be sure that someone is wearing a particular type of armor and calculate how squishy they might be or how hard/fast they might swing as a result. I wonder if this isn't the "make everybody equally upset" option myself, but it's worth mentioning.
>
>  - Add a Toggle Functionality When Inspecting
>  So when you inspect someone's gear, would you like a button to remove their transmogs in that one window? Well, why not! It doesn't affect the world around us, it serves something of a gameplay purpose, and curious people can just choose to peek and see without actually removing anything from the one they're peeking at. There is a layer of removal between what you're looking at and what I'm presenting to the world.
>
>  - Transmog Toggle Function For Joke Items
>  The idea being that items like brooms and bunny ears etc etc DO have a toggle function associated with them based on item class or something, but "normal" item pieces like Red Bandana Mask or so are not marked as a joke item, and therefore can't be toggled off. Not sure how viable it if from a programming standpoint, but it might just address the slutmog point without striking with a broad brush to fuck with everyone's tmogs regardless of how provacative it is or isn't.
>
>  - Make Those Who Disable Tmogs Visible
>  One of the concerns I've mentioned is we won't know who sees the tmogged set I've made for myself, and therefore that voice of doubt and hesitation in the back of my head is persistent. This approach would make those who turn the option off visible somehow, maybe with a title like the Hardcore'ers have ("Seer"?), an icon by their name, by appearing on the map somehow, or maybe just with a permanent buff to be seen when clicked. Either way, that does make it clear who is seeing what. Still awkward AF if someone has that in an RP situation for whatever reason, but dems the cake. It still helps overall.
>
>  - Tmog Ignore List
>  A compromise where the idea is that you can turn off tmogs for select people rather than just everyone. I don't know how viable this is from a coding perspective, but it does address the slutmog issue if nothing else.
>
>  - A Toggle To Allow
>  Instead of a toggle to just undo all transmogs, the idea proposed was a toggle you yourself check in to let others undo your transmogs. It's a fair one if nothing else, though at risk of personal opinion, I doubt how utilized it would be or how happy anybody would get from it.
>
>  **Honorary Mention:**
>  - U NAYSAYERS R JUST NARCCISIST/BOTS/SOCKACCOUNTS
>  k. Any good faith Yaysayers out there to engage with? Oh hello arguments in the Yaysayer category, nice to see you!
>
>  - People Use Mods Anyway, Same Thing!
>  This one strikes me as bad faith, so I didn't wanna include it under the Yaysayer banner. But I wanted to address the actual argument, so here goes:
>
>  We can't do anything about people who use mods for one, and for another it's a matter of accessibility. Sorta like guns; sure you might be able to lay your hands on one via the black market or so and voila. But tell me which country is easier to get a gun in: United States or Japan? Compare and contrast their numbers of gun owners.
>
>  By a similar token, maybe someone does have a mod that changes everyone on their screen into a naked female Draenei, but how much worse and more common does it get if TWoW decided to make that a standard toggle feature in the interface menu? You see the issue right?
>
>  - It's Been Around For 14+ Years!
>  True, though it's not a very strong argument as it doesn't mean any Yaysayer necessarily like the fact, not does it invalidate why they'd speak up so strongly now when they have a chance at affecting the outcome. I wanted to mention that.
>
>  - Tmogs May or May Not Be Fine, But Illusions Man!
>  People want the option to turn off illusions too, either for immersion purposes, PvP balance or both. They hit on very similar vibes, and they're both connected to the question of IRL money spent to acquire them. I don't disagree they fuck with my roleplay immersion, but I feel it's more an extension of the entire question than a direct addressing of it one way or the other.
>
>  - TWoW Is Just Following Epoch's Lead
>  I guess? Anyway moving on.
>
>  - A Toggle Option Doesn't Remove the Feature!
>  Not outright, and no one is saying that it does, but it does hollow it out and renders it cheap and pointless. See the Naysayer category for further details.
>
>  - Remove Transmogs, Add Outfitter!
>  I saw someone suggest this, and I'm frankly not entirely sure what the difference is. Do you keep the gear in your bags or in some sort of bank-esque tab you can switch in and out of like with dual spec? I donno, but I wanted to leave it in for people to chew on as I can see the theoretical potential.
>
>  - Transmogging Can Be Used As A Disguise In PvP!
>  It's true. Warfare is often built upon deception, or ability to hide your means from the other side. It opens up doors for PvP strategy by cleverly disguising what you have. Whether or not this is a good thing here is a question I think you will have to answer, especially if you're a PvPer, which I'm not.
>
>  - Who Cares? Work On Something Else!
>  I sort of can't disagree I think resources should go to other matters?   happy_turtle_head
>  But I'm very "I support the notion, I know nothing of the specifics" about it, so here we are. Maybe more efforts to speed up patch release so I can finally check out Northwind and Dun Kithas? Would be nice~
>
>  - What About Naked People?
>  I don't have a strong opinion myself, but if the devs wanna remove the completely nude pieces that remove underwear and leave your character a barbie, I don't oppose. It has no roleplay value (if you really need to get naked, you can unequip everything down to your underwear and achieve the same result), and it has no aesthetic value. Even from a horny perspective, it's a barbie. You'd be better off looking for porn on the internet. But I can also just move along.
>
>  - The Poll Could've Used More Nuanced Options
>  Like "BGs Only" or the likes. It's a fair point, I think, but we work with what we have.
>
>  - Toggle Could Lead To Harassment of RPers!
>  While an RPer on the Naysaying side, I frankly don't see this one at all. Trolls out to ruin things don't need it and can't effectively weaponize it to mess with us or try to ruin our events. And if it does happen? That's what the Report function exists for. Be sure to take screenshots.
>
>  - Transmogs Are Digital Art
>  Yes, but I also think this argument can be made in the opposite direction. As has been mentioned, standard Classic gear- while often mismatched aesthetically -can create some degree of storytelling in and of itself by showcasing where the player has been and who they've spanked over the course of their journey playing the game. That or they bought it off the Auction House. Either or.
>
>  - And Lastly
>  This fucking meme! I love it and everybody deserve to see it.  satisfied_turtle_head
> > **Valari wrote: Tue Jul 15, 2025 3:30 pm**
> > ![Image](https://img-9gag-fun.9cache.com/photo/aXwZ6Mv_460s.jpg)

While I am sure it took you a while to write this whole thing, you are unfortunately not neutral or objective in your post. The way you summarized the "Yes" voters is very poorly done and you did so in a subtle way to make the arguments seem weak or unjustified, while applying all your focus and bias to the "No" voters' side. You yourself admit to it so your thread summary is not accurate and pointless.

You seem to try to appear as more constructive and well-spoken on the matter but the truth of it is your post was done in bad faith.

Thus, sweeping changes across both servers are not a good idea. Because Nordanaar is much bigger, they will dominate every poll. Polls should be directed separately for each community.
>
>  On another note, I struggle to understand how so many can be against this option. If it did exist, the vast majority of those that love transmog will want to see the transmog of other players, and because the majority of the server likes transmog to an extent, only a small fraction of the population would turn it off. The vast majority of RPers and transmog-enjoyers will still enjoy each other's transmog. How does it matter to you that 100 % of playerbase can see your transmog, vs. maybe 80 % of the playerbase? You will still be getting more than plenty attention for your chosen outfit.
>
>  In this case (as opposed to something like a level boost), giving others the freedom to opt out doesn’t diminish your experience, it simply respects theirs.

Because you wouldnt know. Are the togglers the 10% of the population? Are they 20%? Are they 45% as the poll? Is all of the raid group togglers? Why would I waste my time going to MC for the 100th time to hunt for that specific transmog if i dont even know if it will be real.

With the toggle, transmog is not removed, but it is watered down. And that's why you pay good money for a good wine - cause you know that it hasnt been watered down - you are sure about it.

## Post 140278 by Elesion (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-07-18T10:19:00Z

How is this helpful to the discussion? The take-away from this debate should be that there are view points that differ from your own and we should strive to accept them, if not understand them.

If you go back some pages there's heaps of vehement "no" proponents admitting there's issues with some transmogs. But they would rather see them dealt with through rules and GM intervention rather than through a toggle. So even substantial amounts of "your" side in this debate agree that you should not be forced to look at *every* transmog. Even the real world has some rules and laws so you don't have to view everything someone else might want to force onto you. This game is meant to be a light-hearted safespace to escape reality, why should I let my enjoyment be marred by others' questionable choices?

Like in most things - no extreme will ever be the best solution. Not "ban transmogs" and not "everything goes, suck it".

## Post 140285 by Fundead — 2025-07-18T10:43:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140285#p140285 | page 18 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Annihilaki wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 9:19 am**
> It is essentialy the same my friend. A toggle essentialy turns transmog into a glorified client side mod, but with immense in-game cost to acquire.

No. If you have ever played OSRS on Runelite you would understand. There are two plugins that mimic this situation but are client based.

 1) Entity hider, that just hides everbody except you. You can just switch off seing other players. That is client based as nobody else is bothered by me not seeing them and nobody really knows who is using it as its client based. It's really good for some busy minigames and so on.

 2) Transmog that is client based, you can just choose to visually wield whatever gear there is in a game, nobody else in the game can see it or knows what I my gear looks for me. There is no official transmog in OSRS.

All the OSRS ''RP'' is done as fashionscape just by wielding the items you want to display in game for others to see.

The difference is that on TWOW transmog is server based, so everybody who wants (not only you) can see your transmog and that will not change.

Yes, less people may be, but I explained many times why that is irrelevant as population numbers change by time, by server, by horde/alliance and so on.

<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140287#p140287 | page 18 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Ardaran Feralsoul wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 10:31 am**
> This is helpful because its tossing out completely bunk arguments and exposing them for what they are. The arguments that you're describing have unironically *devolved* to the point where people are using words like "forced" and *being treated as being oppressed* by transmog. There's definitely arguments for a "yes" here - I don't buy them and or think they hinge on things that shouldn't be a priority like PvP - but we're past that now.
>
>  Anyway, why does *your* light-hearted safe space to escape reality get to trump mine? Lets be real and revisit this again. It doesn't, that's the real answer. You don't get to undermine something that's mine (by extension, a lot of other people's agency since this poll flipped day two and has stayed at a solid 8% "no") (also obligatory lol @ democratizing and jeopardizing the liberties and powers of individuals via tyranny of the masses, good lord, at least I think they tried to mean well).
>
>  I've literally been in this thread since the topic dropped, so, sorry, I'm not really interested in understanding that viewpoint*, let alone accepting it, because its plainly invalid. Asterisk being, again, we're at the point its just silly now. No one is being "forced" to see a transmog. There isn't an execution of force here not anymore than my parody examples on any of the pages. I've purposefully been heightening the outlandishness of my conversation as people get more irrational about why "yes" is apparently so needed. We're at the point it can't be lampooned.

So, I think this is a sign of our times, where politics have mostly devolved into tribalism and nobody listens to the other sides anymore or tries to find a consensus. But I genuinely haven't seen a lot of bad-faith arguments so far. Just differing perspectives. WoW is a sandbox, it's open to many very different playstyles - from the raider to the PvPer, from the RPer to the ganker, from the crafter to the grinder, from the trader to the dungeon runner. And every single one of those people *cares*, cares about the game, cares about their enjoyment of it. This is a really awesome sign about the community and how invested people are in this silly 20-year-old game. But of course this will lead to clashes on policy debates like this. But it shouldn't be seen as hostility or bad-faith trolling, it's just people looking out for themselves, for their own enjoyment (it's a game - enjoyment should trump everything else).

To me "being seen" is not really a valid interest, while "having to see" is something I find incredibly intrusive. But reading the pages of posts here have taught me, that there's people who genuinely feel it's the opposite - being seen is the most important aspect, why having to see is no concern at all.
There will be no convincing either side, since this is about a very personal experience. But we should at the end arrive at a state where we can accept the other point as equally valid and try to work towards a compromise solution together, instead of name-calling and ridiculing the genuine and heart-felt experiences of others.

But I'm gonna be upset if the TWoW team starts handing that mod out like candy to anyone who wants it with a readily accessible click of a button and now, while the use of that mod is effectively encouraged, I gotta go around constantly wondering if I'm a Draenei or not.

The vast majority of RPers and transmog-enjoyers will still enjoy each other's transmog. How does it matter to you that 100 % of playerbase can see your transmog, vs. maybe 80 % of the playerbase? ## Post 140334 by Elesion (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-07-18T14:49:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140334#p140334 | page 19 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Annihilaki wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 2:28 pm**
> But the solution to this is targeted removal of problematic skins, not the nuclear option.

I think targeted removal of certain looks is probably the most universal agreement in this thread, and also possibly the least likely to happen. Akalix has said in the past that monetization comes above everything else and is allowed to break rules or immersion. So while it's nice to see large parts of the community united on this front, I'm not getting my hopes up.

Just to clarify though, nobody's talking about "the nuclear option". The transmog system should not be disabled and apart from a single digit number of people nobody has suggested as much, certainly nobody working for the server.

So transmogging will remain possible, regardless of how this proposal shakes out.
Transmogging will continue to function the exact same for you, regardless of how this proposal shakes out. You will see your character transmogged. You will see others transmogged.
All that's being debated is a toggle that allows people to opt out of the entire system for their local view of the game world. I would hardly call that "the nuclear option". But this leads back to something that has already been shown to be an irreconcilable difference - to me the point of transmogging is making my character pretty for myself, to others the point is to show themselves in a certain way to strangers. But even accepting the second view as a premise - I could never ever see more than 20% of people using that toggle (which should absolutely be default on), so 5-10% of people getting the ability to opt out of seeing transmogs still hardly qualifies as "the nuclear option" as 90% nothing will change at all.

Still, you raise a great point - we should use what little unity we have and push for rules or technical solutions against certain skins/looks/items/transmogs.

## Post 140336 by Ardaran Feralsoul — 2025-07-18T15:02:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140336#p140336 | page 19 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

On a more "serious" note, or at least as serious a note as a discussion about a game can be, this issue I haven't seen brought up anywhere here yet - maybe I overlooked it.

A compromise suggests meeting in the middle. That sounds all nice and happy, its stuff we were taught when we were kids. But really, for all the people calling for compromise, there seems to be a pretty, seemingly, unintentional neglect of the fact that any sort of compromise comes at the cost of those who say "no" whereas those who say "yes" only stand to gain. What, exactly, do any of us in opposition get out of this change that benefits us to change our minds?

Sure, you can call that selfish - and honestly, whatever, I don't really care even if it is - but be real about this, as difficult as that can be.

If the "yes" magically wins at this point, or we just get overrode and this was all politicking and theatre, the "no" people only lose. If we, the "no" people, compromise at all, we still lose. Why would it logically stand then for the "no" crowd to want to do this? Its nothing but a detriment at that point. Someone could raise their hand and say "PvP!" but cool, okay, granted. Neat. What else? That's it? So the even smaller percentage of RPers and transmoggers out there who are avid PvPers - mind you, people were basically saying even combined, both these parties don't matter at all *in this very thread* - are the "winners" for the "nos?"

Food for thought because there's no convincing, beneficial argument otherwise. This entire argument conceptually is biased in favor of the "yes" party because they only stand to gain, whether even a fraction of the suggestions happen or the whole thing does.

Again, "selfish" but let's go low here. Pretty basic reasoning to reflect on the fact at the end of the day, people are looking out for their own interests first (and should) but there's an obvious disparity in the nuances of the argument.

## Post 140344 by Zacharis — 2025-07-18T15:39:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140344#p140344 | page 19 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

This is a tricky one and I understand why its so closely divided. I like transmog and I like that its in turtle wow. Gear stats being locked to looks results in some pretty funky looking characters that wouldnt be the norm in any fantasy world. The option to change your looks without sacrificing stats especially if locked to specific armor types is a good thing imo

That being said this is a classic server and the original game did not have this feature. It does have consequences in being able to determine how well geared someone is pvp  and how the world esthetic has always looked in general for classic wow. I think a toggle is the fairest way to deal with this.

The consequence of this is that the world becomes a bit less defined and more subjective. The biggest draw for classic wow for me is that it is more of a world to escape to and less of a videogame and so this shouldnt be ignored. I think this is a big draw for many people as well. However the truth is it is a video game with mods it was always a subjective experience. You cant control how others see you.

For me I would just keep it toggled on and from what I can tell the vast majority of others would too. My feeling is the majority of yes votes aren't people that would toggle it off but only feel its fair to have the option for others

## Post 140351 by Elesion (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-07-18T16:06:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140351#p140351 | page 19 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Annihilaki wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 3:33 pm**
> Transmog will have no purpose if the toggle happens - people might still ocassionally do it for certain armor pieces for while inside raids, but mark my words - in cities nothing will change, you will still see the nakeds. Everyone will auto equip altsets with Armor Rack.

I think that's a fair point, that the outliers who want to create outrage with their looks will still do it through equipping the items. And that should for sure be addressed imo.
But the argument cuts both ways - using your logic, everyone can still keep their RP look the same way and using the actual items instead of transmogs it would force everyone to see you the same as well, regardless of their toggle state. Problem solved? Of course not, just mentioning that the problem is *always* more complicated than one can make it out to be.
There will be no true fits-all solution. We're talking statistics. And as usual the people who will care a lot will find ways around any kind of technical limitation. And the vast majority of people who don't really care, will just leave it on default.

> **Annihilaki wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 3:33 pm**
> I have not heard a single post for someone complaining about mogs in raids. I read every single post in this thread. So the change is pointless, hurtful, and fueled by a minority of vanilla transmog haters who managed to make a big portion of people who are indifferent come to their side because "more options more good".

See, that's disheartening. Because there are 4-5 such posts and they start on the very first page. There's a few people raising the argument that they hate someone with a farmer transmog wailing on KT or someone in a bikini tanking Nefarian. That's not necessarily how I feel about immersion, but a blanket statement that nobody has raised this (they have) and that you're read every post (you haven't) doesn't really help anyone here. We're talking about a diverse group of tens of thousands of players. Any opinion you can think of is probably genuinely held by someone on the server.
This is not a "either you're with us or against us" tribalist issue where there's only 2 camps. The vote is fake anyway, Jamey said as much in the first post. I could stuff in more votes using my alt accounts, but it really doesn't matter. This is not a contest of "either/or", this is about gauging what the community thinks about this issue and finding a solution that makes people happy. And personally, yeah, giving a (small) set of players an option to experience the game like it used to be in 2006, an option they previously have not had, is worth adding. They gain something tangible that was impossible previously. The flipside is that avid transmoggers lose something they have no real way of even knowing about (without the other player straight up mentioning their look). Not a perfect solution, but probably a decent one. Again, I understand that some people feel very differently about this issue. This is clearly a problem where not everyone can fully get what they want.

## Post 140369 by Avendor — 2025-07-18T19:32:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140369#p140369 | page 19 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Fundead wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 11:53 am**
> Basically it's a matter of accessibility and encouragement. I can't stop you or anyone from downloading a mod that turns me into a naked female Draenei, that's something I gotta live with. But I'm gonna be upset if the TWoW team starts handing that mod out like candy to anyone who wants it with a readily accessible click of a button and now, while the use of that mod is effectively encouraged, I gotta go around constantly wondering if I'm a Draenei or not.

Ok, I got you. So if transmog people side is fine with it not being toggle switch in game, but addon, the end result is the same for everybody and that is even possible to make, than I guess it is totally good option and could be great to consider so everybody is happy while playing.
[/quote]
I'm not quite following your grammar, I'd ask you to rewrite it.
But what I *think* you're saying, correct me if I'm wrong, is "So if people can do the same thing through a different source and achieve the same end result, you're okay with it?"

Working off that assumption, the answer is.. No, not really. But it's an unenforceable proposition and it's not reasonable to put the burden of trying to enforce that on my shoulders. If I could I would in an instant take those mods away for an RP server (not PvP or pure PvE), but I can't so I'm kind of just living with it. So why can't I do the same with a toggle function? Because it's the same problem on a much larger scale. I would have to try and live with that too, but it'd be much harder on me and feels like a losing battle upfront.

> **Atreidon wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 2:14 pm**
> We already have different "truths" for different players in the form of hd mod, client side model replacers and the likes.
>
>  You have no control of how other players see you anyway.
>
>  If you wanna fight tooth and nail against this "segregation" you would have to forbid the sizeable part of the playerbase that uses hd textures from doing so, as they outright replace certain pieces of gear with something that does look nothing alike

Aaaaaa! This talking point about HD mods and whatnot just will not go away even when I spell it out to people in two long, detailed posts why those don't quite compare in the RP Naysayer's mind! Did I write too long posts trying to explain it? Did it get lost in the weeds and people just gloss over it? *I don't know!*

HD mods. Do not change the model of my equipment. It makes my character look more Pixar.
Other mods. May or may not, but we can't do much about that and the issue is that making it far more accessible is gonna exaggerate the problem by I don't even know how many times over, cuz now everyone can get it and get it **conveniently**, so the issue is farmore wide-spread and harder to try and ignore.

If you think it's still worth adding a toggle feature in spite that and that Naysayers should suck it up, then okay! That's fine, even if I obviously disagree! But **please** acknowledge the points being made by the other side when you argue against it. Pleeeease.

> **Karrados wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 2:18 pm**
> You will still look transmogged to yourself and anyone that keeps the option on but I am growing rather tired of all the Fetish/Kink Transmogs that are turning up recently including Bunnygirls and just naked people in general.

> **Elesion wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 2:49 pm**
> So transmogging will remain possible, regardless of how this proposal shakes out.
>  Transmogging will continue to function the exact same for you, regardless of how this proposal shakes out. You will see your character transmogged. You will see others transmogged.

> **Elesion wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 4:06 pm**
> But the argument cuts both ways - using your logic, everyone can still keep their RP look the same way and using the actual items instead of transmogs it would force everyone to see you the same as well, regardless of their toggle state.

crying_turtle
Wall of separation point. And adding a toggle to address slutmogs is going in with a hammer for a scalpel problem.

> **Elesion wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 2:49 pm**
> > **Annihilaki wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 2:28 pm**
> > But the solution to this is targeted removal of problematic skins, not the nuclear option.
>
>   I think targeted removal of certain looks is probably the most universal agreement in this thread, and also possibly the least likely to happen. Akalix has said in the past that monetization comes above everything else and is allowed to break rules or immersion. So while it's nice to see large parts of the community united on this front, I'm not getting my hopes up.
>
>  Just to clarify though, nobody's talking about "the nuclear option". The transmog system should not be disabled and apart from a single digit number of people nobody has suggested as much, certainly nobody working for the server.

I agree calling it "nuclear" is a little over-the-top. But the point remains, the toggle function would hit all of us even if we survive the blast. Again, it's a hammer solution against a scalpel problem at best, and a hammer solution in search of a problem at worst.

> **Ardaran Feralsoul wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 3:02 pm**
> A compromise suggests meeting in the middle. That sounds all nice and happy, its stuff we were taught when we were kids. But really, for all the people calling for compromise, there seems to be a pretty, seemingly, unintentional neglect of the fact that any sort of compromise comes at the cost of those who say "no" whereas those who say "yes" only stand to gain. What, exactly, do any of us in opposition get out of this change that benefits us to change our minds?

Much as we're in the same Naysayer camp, I definitely think there are fair compromises that can be made here. Like disabling during BG's where gameplay is far more important and RP is probably not gonna be very relevant. Why not? Or the idea of Tel'Abim being changed, but Nordanaar staying unchanged. I don't see how everyone wouldn't be happy with that one. What am I missing?

> **Annihilaki wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 3:33 pm**
> I have not heard a single post for someone complaining about mogs in raids. I read every single post in this thread. So the change is pointless, hurtful, and fueled by a minority of vanilla transmog haters who managed to make a big portion of people who are indifferent come to their side because "more options more good".

Easy with the name-calling, it's not helping our case or showing us to be better than the guy who called me bad faith in an instance without even attempting a "hey, you got this wrong/missed this" or else providing his own list.

> **Atreidon wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 3:34 pm**
> The only difference between a mod and a toggle ingame is accessability.
>  You yourself are having a hd mod. I personally believe my tauren looks hideous and goofy with hd mod. But you want my tauren to look this way. And thats fine. You look at it, not me.

This is true. But that matter of accessibility is kind of a big deal for us.
As for the HD mod, the difference is that it doesn't change my gear, so it's not as egregious. If I show up with my High Elf in front of Annihilaki's Tauren, he's gonna see blue robes; odds of exception are very small. If the toggle is implemented, there is- as far as I can tell at any given moment -a 50/50 chance he's instead gonna see red T1 robes. Which don't look *bad* in this case mind you, but it's not the look I wanna present and I don't like the ultra-glowing Benediction or rave party T2 head Priests get.

> **Zacharis wrote: Fri Jul 18, 2025 3:39 pm**
> However the truth is it is a video game with mods it was always a subjective experience. You cant control how others see you.

I overall appreciate your post, feels very chill and genuine in what's starting to feel like a mire to me.
I will focus on the part of the quote I wanna respond to though:
It's *true*, but we can keep it from being more wide-spread. In a previous list, I used the example of gun accessibility. You can find that on the previous thread page, but TL;DR compare gun ownership in the US vs. Japan. And ah, read the rest of my post here. I address again why it's an issue to add toggle vs. relying on mods.

<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140418#p140418 | page 20 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

19 pages of discussion with many arguments, counterarguments, reasons why a toggle will improve gameplay or ruin it, why won't make a difference or why it will. Interesting read, certainly some reasoning here that I'd never have thought of. But for me, it seems like both sides can be summarized into two key arguments:
- Yes, add the toggle, because I want to be in control of *how I see myself and others*

- No, don't add the toggle, because I want to be in control of *how others see me*
To me, the first of these sounds the most reasonable: give players the ability to choose for themselves, rather than restricting the choices of others. But it's clear that this is a far more heated subject than I'd ever have guessed, so good luck to the mods in making the best (or least bad) decision.

- No, don't add the toggle, because I like transmog in vanilla

- If toggle happens - the no-people will get super butt-hurt, a lot of them will stop, revenue from costumes will surely dip, and the whining in forums will be immeasurable.

- If toggle does not happen - the yes-people will not care, they never actually did, they will go around enjoying their pvp and raids, SW will still be full of afkers in programmer socks and bunny ears, and all will be back to normal.

Firstly this discussion is not a about a server that will launch in future, it is a change to a server(-community) that is established. For many people that's too much to comprehend and for them everything starts at 0 if they feel like it.

TWoW started a masquerade ball and invites everybody to take part in it even makes a months long feature on the server introduces items only purchased through a donation and signaling to everybody THIS is a core feature for this server.
And now 6 months later it's just opt-in?
So maybe in the future half the server filters out transmogs?  Maybe just open a transfer to Tel'Abim for 2-4 weeks and split the community for real and let people that like the original TWoW vision have their way. On TA they can implement the toggle because PvP and stuff and everybody is happy.

Imho instead of this they should fix the barber shop and the naked custom-skins with no underwear.

Besides yes or no: This whole thing is a bad look for TWoW. Like the with they way they did the transmog-contest it seems the people in the PR department do not know what they are doing. :(

## Post 140491 by Fundead — 2025-07-19T13:47:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140491#p140491 | page 20 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Annihilaki wrote: Sat Jul 19, 2025 1:08 pm**
> I bet your mind does impossible mathematical equations the moment you spot someone's gear from afar so you can make out if you can tank 2.4 or 3 of his hits with that exact amount of strength from all items. Clown reasoning.  Everyone counts power level by max hp, always have done.
>
>  edit: We are with you (pvpers) in this matter btw. We dont care about a toggle in pvp areas.

I am totally not elitist pvp'er and prolly never will be. I like pve more lately.
I have not yet played a lot of bgs and other pvp here at lvl60, but from other expansions like TBC and WOTLK, that I have spent years in pvping in bgs, arenas and world. Those had visible pvp and pve gearsets and weps, than I would say you are completely wrong. You can never and never have just counted power of somebody from hp and mana.

If the wep they are using has some chance on hit effects. If warrior is using mace, he can stun you, with sword and axe there is no such effect and so on. On wep enchants there is even visual effects so you can see those and make predictions.

You don't need to have math equations rolling in your mind because of opponents gear, you can just judge the situation and with time its automatic, like driving a car.

The inspect option is only viable in duels, you can inspect somebody before and hope he will not change stuff around when you stop the inspect. Inspect and all the rest can never work in any other pvp scenario, as I mentioned before its not chess with long time format, decision must be made asap.

## Post 140500 by Grinface — 2025-07-19T15:00:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140500#p140500 | page 20 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I am all for letting players decide if they want to see transmogged gear or not. Let the players decide what kind of classic+ experience they want. For me personally transmogrification is one of the few things about TWOW that I don't care for simply because I think mismatched looking clown suit of a gear set is just part of Vanilla/Classic experience. I also think it's just cool to see and recognize the gear someone is wearing and know what the set or weapon they use does. And as some people have pointed out; transmogrification can be used to fool players in PvP by disguising your gear. I am not a PvP:er myself but I can definitely see their point and think it's valid.

But I also understand that people like certain tier sets or just want to look cool, funny or even slutty wearing pieces of gear they like. I think having the option to toggle transmogrification on or off is a good compromise where both sides can still enjoy the game the way they want.

## Post 140544 by Grinface — 2025-07-19T21:54:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140544#p140544 | page 21 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Annihilaki wrote: Sat Jul 19, 2025 9:23 pm**
> You are being disingenuous now. Would YOU buy it if you knew half of the people couldn't even see it? Answer thruthfully, and you got your answer.

Yes. If the price is right.

Because I don't care if every single player sees it. For all we know most players may not even toggle transmogging off but they still like to have the option. But honestly I don't care about transmogging, much less buying transmog gear or fashion coins using real money. Because I like to earn my cool looking gear, or coins, in game. If I ever bought anything from the store it'd be gameplay related stuff like bags or portable auctioneer or mailbox.

If I was a serious roleplayer then I'd want roleplayers I play with to see my transmogged gear. And they most certainly would have it toggled on. Non-roleplayers who're essentially playing their own game don't matter to me.

There's my truthful answer.

> **Annihilaki wrote: Sat Jul 19, 2025 9:26 pm**
> It's Vanilla+ not vanilla. Thus the need and want for transmog. That silly transmog toggle was never heard before other than upcoming Epoch. Guess why? Cause it makes no sense.

If it makes "no sense" why Epoch decided to add it as an option? Clearly they thought it "made sense" to them because they want to give players the option to choose their Classic+ experience. Maybe people have suggested something like it for TWOW before? Or didn't because they thought it couldn't be done on TWOW's current client. But what I do know, and as the poll here demonstrates, is that there's a lot of people who don't like transmogrification and don't think it's part of Classic(+), being one of the many modern retail features brought in Cataclysm.

But at this point I'm just gonna end this with: agree to disagree. I've said my piece. This isn't a make or break kind of deal to me anyway. Besides the poll result seems to be quite clear and the toggle won't be added assuming the devs go with the majority opinion. Congrats.
