## Post 140570 by Zulkord — 2025-07-20T01:13:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140570#p140570 | page 21 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Annihilaki wrote: Sat Jul 19, 2025 11:11 pm**
> > **Fundead wrote: Sat Jul 19, 2025 11:05 pm**
> > Last dungeon I tanked I got some good comments about my tanking, that was nice to hear. In almost 20 years of playing wow (with some pauses in between) I have never received comments about my visual looks.. Nobody have bothered...
> >  If I would look like  female draenei on somebodies screen its better than male I guess, but I have no feelings about it really as I don't have to look at it and don't really know how it looks.
>
>   It feels equally nice when someone whisps about your transmog! Happens a lot in retail wow and ff14!

To both of you:
Tmog compliments happen a lot in TWoW too. Sometimes indirect. Even being refered to as "The paladin (tank) in black" in BWL pug with barely any roleplayers feels much nicer and more personal than "The paladin in T2", and it is invigorating.

To the general argument:
If you don't care how other characters look - you don't care about implementing the toggle.
If you don't *like* how other characters look - well, with this toggle like it is worded in the poll, you can turn off scarse awful tmogs which are mostly concentrated we all know where, but with doing that you also turning off all great and immersive (in story and setting sense, not in clown Vanilla mismatched bis estetic sense) tmogs around you.

This is MMO, its core is interaction between people. Dividing people undermines it. If you say "why do you care how do you look on my screen? It's my screen!" - well, I care because I interact with you. If you don't like my tmog and don't want to interact with me because of it - you can choose to not interact with me and not look at it, but you can not force me to look differently. That's the purpose of the feature - to be seen. If I wanted to look at cutsie elvie with big boobs for my own pleasure - I would use a mod for myself, not built-in feature to change my appearance for everyone in the game.

To devs: people interacting tend to bring other people to the project to interact with and/or to keep ther newly acquired friends here. People who want to cut interactions to the minimum and boil the experience down to gear and numbers tend to play some kind of single player game and don't contribute to the long-term growth of community.

When there was no transmog, people would insta-swap RP costumes out of combat. Later in Vanilla, that was automated with addons like Armor Rack. Transmog made it easier (you don't need to carry all these alt-costumes), and made it so you can keep the RP during combat.

If the toggle ever came to pass, RP people would ditch the transmog in support of the old system. Result being, nothing would change for you. Still poisoned eyes. You are just punishing a whole community, and the slutmoggers would not even butt an eye.

## Post 140623 by Ardaran Feralsoul — 2025-07-20T15:00:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140623#p140623 | page 22 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Hey there, me again, here to ruin things for people.

So, fun fact about online surveys is that its [generally considered pretty good](https://www.surveylab.com/blog/what-is-a-good-response-rate-for-a-survey) for an *external survey* to get 20 to 25% response rate and anywhere from 10 to 30% response rate for an *internal survey*... like, oh I don't know, asking an internal community their own thoughts.

Doing some quick math here, a total of around 1200 responses from here and the Discord is...

~14% of the entire community, which hey, look at that! That matches the expected internal survey rate of 10 to 30%, in fact. But hey, why stop there, right? Let's pretend the active community is 10000 people and - oh look, its still above 10% at ~12%! Still considered acceptable.

[Another fun fact, for this survey, you would only need a statistical sample of around 385 out of the actual 10000 or so population (people active on Turtle total) to get a 95% confidence that the survey is valid.](https://www.cloudresearch.com/resources/guides/statistical-significance/determine-sample-size/)

Even better, you only would need a +/-5% difference in the results! At 8% that's over 50% more of a determination of what you need. Neat.

Anyway, I'm not sponsored or anything by my source but hey, whatever.

The devs got a good turnout on their survey and the results are very realistic. If you don't like that, sorry. /shrug

## Post 140648 by Ardaran Feralsoul — 2025-07-20T18:23:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140648#p140648 | page 22 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Gonna be real here, my mans.

Yeah, you probably shouldn't rely on a small section of the population as the de facto representation of the whole by gauging forum outrage.*

 * Except where you can create a data set that fits the criteria for statistical significance. Its really, really, really convenient that this happened to be a poll and the actual data set to represent the population is pretty accurate. *You quite literally got in the ballpark of three times the minimum you would need* in responses between here and the Discord to be representative.

Sure, I'll concede there's an ever narrowing window where the actual results lie outside the confidence interval (roflmao, kek even) but at that point you're gunning for a less than 1% chance these aren't representative. I don't know if you've ever farmed anything in this game ever but we all know how rare anything sub 1% really is. I'm pretty sure the 8% "no" vote is accurate for something *this silly*.

Part of the reason this worked (as in, met criteria to be considered valid in results) is because they broadcasted this survey to the total active population. If you've logged in, in the past week, somewhere around 1 out of 10 to 1 out of 12 people, from the look of it, responded to this in some way. And while we have to chuck out some of the results like all the random emojis, people accidentally voting for the wrong things, changing their minds and so on...

... all of that's already included in the statistical analysis.

Again, we couldn't ask for a better scenario to see what people actually think. Even if we chopped off the top 10% of "no" votes - this has a name and I can't be bothered to go look it up - this would still be within that needed 5% range.

I'm sorry, plainly put, you're wrong. People, by distinct majority, with >99% certainty, do not want this change.

## Post 140728 by Ardaran Feralsoul — 2025-07-21T11:02:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140728#p140728 | page 22 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Seltsamuel wrote: Mon Jul 21, 2025 8:39 am**
> Why is "everyone" assuming that anyone would immediately go into the options and toggle this on? The PvP crowd will certainly do it, so they can finally again use game knowledge to discern the items their opponent is wearing. But the others? Really? Having more options is always nice so it is a yes from me.

I know this is a controversial statement in <insert current year> but *more isn't always better*.

Now that everyone is done clutching their pearls and gasping and those who have fainted are conscious again, this is something I wanted to say pages ago but didn't want to interject when more pressing comments were circulating. Now that I'm here though, here's the issue.

Part of what makes a game consistent and uniform is... a sense of consistency and uniformity. If I can assume you're seeing what I am seeing and vice versa, we're both experiencing the same world. But if you're going around manipulating that (be it Turtle endorsed like this toggle idea or through addons), we're no longer seeing the same world. Sure, sure, whatever, old argument of "But your red isn't my red, so it isn't the same red!" from like 2015 or whatever; bunk argument, the red's the same, the reality of that red is the same, but our *perception* of it is different.

Goes the same for transmog. If you see my character transmogged, you're fundamentally seeing the same thing but how you're perceiving it varies. If you get to just uncheck it, we're no longer in the same world. The more we keep doing this with things in the game, the more the experience breaks down.

Would it make sense to let mages, priests, warlocks, druids, and or shamans get a spell that lets them see through illusions, disguises, and transmog they can cast as a buff on themselves and others that lasts 10 minutes? Sure, that's a cool, in universe way of doing things. Transmog is just a glamer anyway, a type of illusion magic. Its functional and has a reason to exist in the world. You can make that work, it can apply to anyone, it can be a gold sink to train the basic version at 50+, then have a reagent (then imagine the group buff book being stupidly rare and a raid only drop from certain magic based bosses, ooooOOOOooooOOO we're combining lore and mechanics, spooky!) (P.S. think of all the cool things the devs could hide with that alone that would make exploring even more worthwhile. Sure you might need some friends to do that but lets be honest, its a niche spell, and Vanilla has a ton of those.)

A button is about as game-y as it gets.

There's solutions to this problem that can exist *in the game* at the same time as being *in the world of the game* without falling into "more is better."

 *If* it has to exist at all.

## Post 140744 by Visagezero — 2025-07-21T15:34:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140744#p140744 | page 22 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

My personal take on the matter is that customizing your character the way you want to be seen is a core part of an MMORPG game. Transmogrification isn’t just a cosmetic feature — it’s a vital form of personal expression in MMORPGs. In a game like Turtle WoW, which places such a strong emphasis on player-driven storytelling, immersion, and community, transmog is one of the only tools players have to visually communicate their character's identity, history, and roleplay style.

In an MMORPG, your character’s appearance is part of their story. Whether you’re a scarred veteran in mismatched armor, a noble knight in pristine silver, or a rogue covered in shadows, your outfit signals to others what kind of character you’re playing. Transmog allows players to curate that visual story without being shackled to the look of whatever high-stat gear they happen to be wearing.

Disabling other players’ transmog is effectively erasing their visual storytelling — their effort to craft a persona, a theme, or an aesthetic within the world. It sends a message that their identity doesn’t matter to you, even in a shared space meant for interaction and roleplay.

The request to disable transmog visibility often stems from a desire for “authenticity” — wanting to see what gear people are really wearing. But that’s a meta concern, not a world-building one. If someone needs to know your actual gear, they can inspect you. No one is harmed by seeing another player wearing a set they’ve chosen to represent their character.

What does hurt the community, though, is the idea that one person’s preference to view others "accurately" should override someone else’s effort to be visually expressive in a shared space.

Disabling transmogs for others undermines what makes MMORPGs unique: shared experience, creativity, and connection. Transmog is a player’s way of saying, “This is who I am in this world.” Taking that away — even optionally — chips away at the soul of what makes Turtle WoW special.

## Post 140747 by Ardaran Feralsoul — 2025-07-21T15:43:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140747#p140747 | page 22 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Grinface wrote: Mon Jul 21, 2025 3:25 pm**
> I actually really like this idea. Just make the buff last more than mere 10 minutes. 2 hours sounds much more reasonable.

Thank you! The reason I say a 10 minute buff with a reagent is, is there's a cost to time and opportunity. Recasting it is a pain, by design, but its worth having up when you need it (PvP). Limiting it to mages (duh) or priests (equal duh), and druids or paladins and shamans with their own versions (for balance reasons) feels authentic (paladins are known to see through lies, shamans see the world for what it is, etc). It also is costly because spamming it is going to cost you reagents. Making it a magical buff also means it can be purged or dispelled (also its just an inherently magical thing, so it makes sense it can be subject to those effects). It also falls in line with how a lot of Vanilla buffs did, where a lot were 10 minutes, with a greater, party version for 30 minutes. It would have a really legitimate feel to it.

If this is something "Turtle WoW *has* to do," lets at least do it in a way that adds to the entire game. You wouldn't really see it outside of PvP too and it wouldn't be worth doing outside of PvP (barring niche novelty uses like I said earlier, which are fine, such as using it to protect better buffs on Garr or find stuff the devs hid in the world).

Is it a compromise? Sure. But my point is, is that this is just a way to do the same thing a whole lot better if people are so dedicated to having this exist. The whole "more is better and we need more options and more buttons to toggle" feels really, really awful.

Anyway, I'm all done! Poll's basically over. Hope the devs got feedback that's helpful.

I can tell you why the ''everyone seeing the same world'' is BS.

We look at things differently. You are visual transmog person, I am not. You have had some good experience with that, I have not, as I do not really care about that. We pay attention to different things. For example you look at visual aspect of the things around you, I look at engineering and design or even production and cost of the same things, as of my engineering background. You look at car based on extras, how its family friendly, I don't care about that, I look at how centered mass of the car is and if it has manual transmission and LSD. So I can look at you and I will anyway think something totally different. I will think nothing really I guess most of the times as I have a lot of other things in my mind, as most of us do.

Same as people mentioned before, for me vanilla experience (as I played it back than) is without transmog with everybody showing their gear or just some PR clothes they have farmed. I look at people from their gear as that shows me their achievements, not what they farmed in low lvl dungeons or quests, as I care about different things that you.

For everybody you know you are a different person as you have different experience with them and they have different life experience that they base it on.

There is nothing you can do about it or should do about it. There is a saying - for one its wife, for other its daughter and for third its the dress. So for everybody world is different and we see it different based on our life and who we are. If we all would see world the same we all would like the same things and so on, but from this one poll you can see its not that way.

So it does not matter if red for me is red for you as the end result as our thoughts and actions and feelings of it are still different.

For me its about your actions not about your look. For me WOW is about murder not fashion contest. And to make it feel like more murder I like to see everybody as they are, just to keep it real :D

## Post 140817 by Torzag — 2025-07-22T06:28:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140817#p140817 | page 23 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Nobody is voting yes because it breaks immersion. Everyone can play whatever character they wish whether it's a cloud bunny warrior, a puritan priest, a zealot paladin, etc.

For most of those people if you turn transmog off, they will seriously start looking like clowns. Which would break your immersion even more. Especially if they're rocking the 2.5t set from AQ40 for example.

When you log in and select a server, it says "Nordnaar PVE RP" and for this server the devs should focus on PVE and RP. PVP has it's own realm, it's own playerbase. To that effect, PVP is also dead on Nordnaar. So I have no idea why it's being pushed as a good idea.

In fact, since there's so little interest in it, I believe it should be the other way around. If there's a way to make RP/PVE better for the players, it should be added even if the PVP content suffers. Hence why there's a PVP realm to begin with.

Many people in this thread and that have been on the server forever have spoken about RP features such as Outlaw mode being removed because it somehow impedes the PVP crowd. Well, why not bring such features back on the RP realm, and have them disabled on the PVP realm?

What you are saying is just totally stupid. The game does not need to be divided between PVP and PVE as there is nothing that would stop them from existing on the same server (except transmog maybe, dunno), especially with the population we have. I have played on servers with 1k - 1,5k players online max (and it was no crossfaction etc) and it had very healthy pvp and pve player base in the same time. It had bgs running, raids running all parallel to each other. Many of the players enjoy both (like me), why would I make new char on other server, level it to max lvl to play pvp if I can do it all with my main ?

Only difference between pve and pvp server is the world pvp aspect, but with 10k people online you can still find many flagged people. Here its also crossfaction playing, but I dont think it was like that on original blizzard servers and that changes nothing in its core.

If you don't change class spells or core mechanics from server to server to adjust them more for pvp or pve, than whats the difference ? You can make updates that help pvp, than you can make ones that help pve.. one does not cancel each other.

We have 10k people online, you can split it into like 4 separate servers with already very healthy population.

## Post 140892 by Ardaran Feralsoul — 2025-07-22T11:56:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140892#p140892 | page 24 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

It's really funny to me that some of the people ranting and raving the most about "egotistical behavior" in other players are the same ones who are using the argument that trolls voted to swing the poll against them specifically. I'm just going to leave that at that. I think that speaks a lot for itself.

Anyway.

First, no, that didn't happen and like I covered a few pages back, even if you chopped off the top 10% of votes from "no", so like 58+ so votes, "no" still would have crossed the finish line greater than 5% (in reality one side only needed to win by 3% due to how large the poll's actual voters were relative to the population of 10,000 to 12,000); so either the trolls didn't try hard enough or they weren't statistically significant (assuming they existed and somehow influenced >10% the votes of "no").

Second, that's really funny even if they do exist because... Third, related to that, assuming that's what happened at all, have you and your boys ever considered *you're* the problem that people hate you so much they all voted against you just because you voted one way? Not a real question. Its entirely rhetorical, alright? No one needs or wants an answer because that's so hilariously made up as an argument and excuse like a bunch of others from similar mouthpieces it only bears pointing out here, like the past ones, to show its not credible.

Okay, okay, back to the real topic.

"Yes" didn't just lose, "yes" lost by a lot and that's not a coincidence. For "yes" to have won they'd first needed to have made up +/- 100 votes to tie "no", then another 100 or so because that's the difference to 8%. Out of around 1200 responses, "yes" would've needed to garner around 200 of that more than it got (thus taking from "no", not adding more votes to the total) to have representative. It didn't, not even close that's something like being off by over a third, like 35% behind.

I don't know if this is settling in on you, dear reader, but let's make this a little more clear.

This poll only needed ~385 people to be valid and only needed ~10% response rate from the entire active population. It cleared both. Not only did it clear both, it cleared the total participants by *greater than three times its minimum* and had around *50% more a response rate than the minimum*. The margin "no" won is 50% higher than the minimum 5% expected (and almost 300% the actual minimum needed).

Are the numbers sinking in here?

Let's also pretend that the donors are, somehow, magically, just like those pesky RPers, the real problem. Let's also go on the bold assumption that like the pesky RPers, ever donor voted "no." Do you honestly think there's more than 58 some odd donors who all voted no to keep their precious immersion breaking mogs and that "skewed" the vote somehow? Enough that "no" should have lost? Revisit the second paragraph if so. Those maths just ain't mathing, chief.

I know that's not going to stop the babbling here because we have some "forum PvP champs" but here's the case. "Yes" lost because there's a very distinct majority of the Turtle community who do not want this and not only are they a distinct majority, they're apparently a consistent one (this poll went to 8%, "no" favored day two and stayed that way for the rest of the week).

The idea of toggling transmog is not turtle-y enough for the Turtle club, mmk? Most people don't want it. So either 0.1% chance odds (not even joking, its around there) this entire poll was wrong or this is how things *actually* are. People voted against it, plainly, by the numbers, because it does not fit how they want to engage with Turtle. Turtle is not <whatever other server and dev team>, Turtle WoW is Turtle WoW. It should focus on its *own* unique identity and what it brings to the table for *its* community (direct competition also never results in success, so, take a page from biology here too and find a niche you fit into)

## Post 140902 by Torta (Turtle WoW Team) [STAFF] — 2025-07-22T13:25:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140902#p140902 | page 24 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Thanks to everyone who joined the discussion.

Based on what we heard, **we've decided not to add a transmog toggle in the open world**. For many, a character's appearance is part of the RPG experience, whether you're actively roleplaying or simply enjoying your adventure.

 **Illusions and Orb of Deception will be fully banned in battlegrounds and arenas, including any class-specific bugs that force them on.**

A toggle for BGs and arenas is still on the discussion table. We'll be monitoring how many players misuse the feature to negatively impact others' experience. A final decision will be based on those observations.
